Second order energy expansion of Bose gases with three-body interactions
Morris Brooks

Abstract
We provide a second order energy expansion for a gas of N N bosonic particles with three-body interactions in the Gross-Pitaevskii regime. We especially confirm a conjecture by Nam, Ricaud and Triay in [ 22 ] , where they predict the subleading term in the asymptotic expansion of the ground state energy to be of the order N \sqrt{N} . In addition, we show that the ground state satisfies Bose-Einstein condensation with a rate of the order 1 N \frac{1}{\sqrt{N}} .



1
Introduction

In this manuscript we study a dilute Bose gas consisting of
N
quantum particles subject to Bose-Einstein statistics, in which the individual particles interact with each other via a three-body potential

\displaystyle V_{N}(x,y,z):=NV\!\left(\sqrt{N}(x-y),\sqrt{N}(x-z)\right),

(1)

defined in terms of a given bounded and non-negative function
V:\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
with compact support. The quantum gas is then described by the self-adjoint operator

\displaystyle H_{N}:=-\sum_{1\leq k\leq N}\Delta_{x_{k}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j<k\leq N}V_{N}(x_{i},x_{j},x_{k}),

(2)

acting on the space of permutation symmetric functions
L^{2}_{\mathrm{sym}}\!\left(\Lambda^{N}\right)
, where
\Lambda:=\left[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right]^{3}
is the three dimensional torus and we assume that
V_{N}
defined in Eq. (
1
) is permutation symmetric in order to assure that
H_{N}
preserves permutation symmetry. The particular scaling in Eq. (
1
) with the number of particles
N
is referred to as the Gross-Pitaevskii regime and yields a short range, but strong, interaction on the scale
r=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
. This especially means that we are dealing with a dilute gas taking up a volume of the order
Nr^{3}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
. Dilute Bose gases with three-particle interactions have previously been studied in
[
20
,
21
,
22
,
23
,
15
]
, where the leading order asymptotics of the ground state energy in the limit
N\rightarrow\infty
has been established as well as Bose-Einstein condensation (BEC) in the Gross-Pitaevskii regime. Here (BEC) refers to the observation that most of the particles occupy the state with zero momentum. In the Gross-Pitaevskii regime specifically, the leading order term in the asymptotics of the ground state energy

\displaystyle E_{N}:=\inf\sigma(H_{N})=\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+o_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(N\right)

(3)

is proportional to the number of particles
N
with a rather explicit constant
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
, see
[
20
]
. While naive first order perturbation theory would suggest that the constant
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
should be given by
\widehat{V}(0)
, it is due to the singular nature of the scaling in Eq. (
1
) that we cannot ignore the presence of three particle correlations leading to a renormalized constant
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)<\widehat{V}(0)
. In the following we will address a conjecture in
[
22
]
, which claims that the subleading term in the asymptotic expansion of
E_{N}
is proportional to
\sqrt{N}
, see our main Theorem
1
. The contributions to the ground state energy
E_{N}
of the order
\sqrt{N}
arise based on two-particle, three-particle and four-particle correlations in the ground state. As a byproduct from the proof of Theorem
1
, we obtain in addition that the ground state
\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N}
of the operator
H_{N}
satisfies (BEC) with a rate
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
, i.e. we show that the ratio of particles outside the state with zero momentum compared to the total number of particles
N
is of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)
. This is an improvement of the (BEC) result in
[
20
]
, where the authors showed that the ratio is of the order
o_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(1\right)
.

It is worth pointing out that much more is known for Bose gases with two-particle interactions, where the expansion of the ground state energy to second order is well known in the Gross-Pitaevskii regime, the thermodynamic limit and interpolating regimes, see e.g.
[
3
,
7
,
8
,
11
,
12
,
14
,
19
]
. Furthermore, (BEC) is well known for the Gross-Pitaevskii regime and regular enough interpolating regimes, even with an (optimal) rate, see e.g.
[
1
,
2
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
10
,
13
,
17
]
, and the subleading term in the expansion of the ground state energy is known to be of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}(1)
. This resolution of the energy is sharp enough to see the spectral gap, which is of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}(1)
as well. For a Bose gas with three-particle interaction in the Gross-Pitaevskii regime we expect the spectral gap to be of the magnitude
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}(1)
, see the conjecture in
[
22
]
, however the second order expansion of the energy only allows for a resolution of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
, which is not sharp enough to see the spectral gap.

As it is not the goal of this manuscript to optimize in the regularity of
V
, we will assume
V\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{6})
for the sake of convenience (although assuming e.g.
V\in H^{9}(\mathbb{R}^{6})
would certainly be sufficient).

The correct constant
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
in the energy asymptotics Eq. (
3
) can be derived formally by making a translation-invariant ansatz for the correlation structure
\varphi(x-u,y-u)
between three particles at position
x
,
y
and
u
, where
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{6}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Utilizing the matrix

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}:=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}2&1\\
1&2\end{bmatrix}}

and the modified Laplace operator
\Delta_{\mathcal{M}}:=\left(\mathcal{M}\nabla_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\right)^{2}
, let us first express the action of the Laplace operator in relative coordinates as

\displaystyle\left(\Delta_{x}+\Delta_{y}+\Delta_{u}\right)\varphi(x-u,y-u)=(2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}}\varphi)(x-u,y-u).

The energy correction associated to the correlation structure
\varphi
is then given by

\displaystyle\braket{\varphi,(-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}}+V)\varphi}-2\braket{\varphi,V}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\left\{2\big|\mathcal{M}\nabla\varphi(x)\big|^{2}+V(x)\varphi(x)^{2}-2V(x)\varphi(x)\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x,

where
\braket{\varphi,V}
describes the interaction between the condensate wavefunction
\Psi\equiv 1
and
\varphi
. Including the potential energy
\braket{\Psi,V\Psi}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}V(x)\mathrm{d}x
and optimizing in
\varphi
leads to

\displaystyle b_{\mathcal{M}}(V):=\inf_{\varphi\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\left\{2\big|\mathcal{M}\nabla\varphi(x)\big|^{2}+V(x)\!\left|1-\varphi(x)\right|^{2}\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x,

(4)

where
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
refers to the space of functions
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
vanishing at infinity with
|\nabla g|\in L^{2}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
. It has been verified in
[
20
]
that minimizer
\omega
to the variational problem in Eq. (
4
) exist and the (modified) scattering length
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
describes the leading order asymptotics of the ground state energy correctly, see Eq. (
3
). Our main Theorem
1
confirms that the next term in the energy asymptotics in Eq. (
3
) is of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
due to contributions from the three-particle correlation
\omega
, as well as from two-particle and four-particle correlations. Regarding the impact of the correlation structure
\omega
on the
\sqrt{N}
order, we observe in the presence of an additional particle at position
z
further interaction terms between
\omega
and itself of the form

\displaystyle\braket{\omega(y,z),V(x,y)\omega(y,z)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}V(x,y)\omega(y,z)^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z,

\displaystyle\braket{\omega(x,z),V(x,y)\omega(y,z)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}V(x,y)\omega(x,z)\omega(y,z)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

The corresponding energy correction is then given by
\gamma(V)\sqrt{N}
with

\displaystyle\gamma(V):

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}V(x,y)\omega(x,z)\omega(y,z)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}V(x,y)\omega(y,z)^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

(5)

In order to quantify the impact of two-particle correlations, respectively four-particle correlations, we make a translation-invariant ansatz
\xi(x\!-\!u)
with
\xi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
, respectively
\eta(x\!-\!u,y\!-\!u,z\!-\!u)
with
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{9}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
. We observe that the interaction energy of
\xi
and
\eta
with the condensate and the three-particle correlation structure
\omega
is given by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big\langle\xi(x),V(x,y)(1-\omega(x,y))\Big\rangle\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}V(x,y)(1-\omega(x,y))\xi(x)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x,

(6)

\displaystyle\Big\langle\eta(x,y,z),V(x,y)\omega(y,z)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}V(x,y)\omega(y,z)\eta(x,y,z)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

(7)

Including the kinetic energy of
\xi
and optimizing in
\xi
, Eq. (
6
) immediately gives rise to the energy correction
-\mu(V)\sqrt{N}
with the proportionality constant
\mu(V)
defined as

\displaystyle\mu(V):

\displaystyle=\inf_{\xi\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nabla\xi(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}V(x,y)(1-\omega(x,y))\xi(x)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x\right\}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\frac{V_{\mathrm{eff}}(x)V_{\mathrm{eff}}(y)}{8\pi|x-y|}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y,

(8)

where we have introduced the effective two-particle interaction

\displaystyle V_{\mathrm{eff}}:\begin{cases}&\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R},\\
&x\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}V(x,y)(1-\omega(x,y))\,\mathrm{d}y.\end{cases}

Finally, in order to identify the energy contribution due to the presence of four-particle correlations
\eta
, we have to express the kinetic and potential energy of
\eta(x-u,y-u,z-u)
in terms of relative coordinates. For this purpose let us define
\mathbb{V}:\mathbb{R}^{9}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\mathcal{M}_{*}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}):

\displaystyle=V(x_{1},x_{2})+V(x_{1},x_{3})+V(x_{2},x_{3})+V(x_{2}-x_{1},x_{3}-x_{1}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{*}:

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}2&1&1\\
1&2&1\\
1&1&2\end{bmatrix}},

and identify the action of the Laplace operator and the potential on
\eta
as

\displaystyle\left(\Delta_{x_{1}}\!\!+\!\Delta_{x_{2}}\!\!+\!\Delta_{x_{3}}\!\!+\!\Delta_{x_{4}}\right)\eta(x_{1}\!-\!x_{4},x_{2}\!-\!x_{4},x_{3}\!-\!x_{4})

\displaystyle\!=\!(2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}\eta)(x_{1}\!-\!x_{4},x_{2}\!-\!x_{4},x_{3}\!-\!x_{4}),

(9)

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i<j<k\leq 4}\!\!\!V(x_{i}\!-\!x_{k},x_{j}\!-\!x_{k})\eta(x_{1}\!-\!x_{4},x_{2}\!-\!x_{4},x_{3}\!-\!x_{4})

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{V}\eta)(x_{1}\!-\!x_{4},x_{2}\!-\!x_{4},x_{3}\!-\!x_{4}).

(10)

Combining kinetic, potential and interaction energy, see Eq. (
7
), Eq. (
9
) and Eq. (
10
), yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\left\{2\big|\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\eta(x)\big|^{2}+\mathbb{V}(x)\eta(x)^{2}-2f(x)\eta(x)\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x=\mathcal{Q}(\eta)-\mathcal{Q}(0),

where we have introduced the function
f(x,y,z):=V(x,y)\omega(y,z)
and the functional

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\eta):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\left\{2\big|\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\eta(x)\big|^{2}+\mathbb{V}(x)\!\left|\frac{f(x)}{\mathbb{V}(x)}-\eta(x)\right|^{2}\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x.

(11)

Note that
\frac{f}{\mathbb{V}}
is well defined and bounded on the support of
\mathbb{V}
, due to the sign of
V\geq 0
. Consequently the corresponding energy correction is given by
-\sigma(V)\sqrt{N}
with

\displaystyle\sigma(V)\!:=\mathcal{Q}(0)-\inf_{\eta\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{9})}\!\mathcal{Q}(\eta).

(12)

It is content of our main Theorem
1
, that
\gamma(V),\mu(V)
and
\sigma(V)
describe the second order correction to the leading order asymptotics of the ground state energy
E_{N}
in Eq. (
3
), which is of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
. Furthermore, we show that the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
term comes with a non-zero pre-factor for a large class of potentials
V
.

Theorem 1
.

Let
V\in C^{\infty}\!\left(\mathbb{R}^{6}\right)
be a bounded and non-negative function with compact support, such that the function
V_{N}
defined in Eq. (
1
) is permutation symmetric. Furthermore, let
\gamma(V),\mu(V)
and
\sigma(V)\in\mathbb{R}
be as in Eq. (
5
), Eq. (
8
) and Eq. (
12
) respectively, and let
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
be as in Eq. (
4
). Then the ground state energy
E_{N}:=\inf\sigma(H_{N})
satisfies

\displaystyle E_{N}=\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+\Big(\!\gamma(V)-\mu(V)-\sigma(V)\!\Big)\sqrt{N}+O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(N^{\frac{1}{4}}\right).

(13)

Furthermore, there exists a
\lambda(V)>0
, such that for all
0<\lambda\leq\lambda(V)

\displaystyle\gamma(\lambda V)-\mu(\lambda V)-\sigma(\lambda V)<0.

Remark 1
.

While Theorem
1
concerns Bose gases in the ultra-dilute Gross-Pitaevskii regime occupying a volume of the order
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
, the leading order behaviour of the ground state energy per unit volume
e(\rho)
is known in the thermodynamic regime as well as a function of the density
\rho
, see
[
21
]
, and given in analogy to the leading order asymptotics in Eq. (
3
) by

\displaystyle e(\rho)=\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)\rho^{3}+o_{\rho\rightarrow 0}\!\left(\rho^{3}\right).

It is remarkable that the coefficients
\gamma(V)
,
\mu(V)
and
\sigma(V)
from Theorem
1
are defined in terms of variational problems on the unconfined space
\mathbb{R}^{3d}
and do not depend on the boundary conditions of the box
\Lambda^{d}
. Substituting
\rho
with
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
in Theorem
1
we therefore expect the second order expansion of
e(\rho)
, as
\rho\rightarrow 0
, to be given by

\displaystyle e(\rho)=\rho^{3}\left(\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)+\Big(\!\gamma(V)-\mu(V)-\sigma(V)\!\Big)\rho\right)+o_{\rho\rightarrow 0}\!\left(\rho^{4}\right).

This would be in contrast with the second order expansion of a Bose gas with two-body interactions, where in the celebrated Lee-Huang-Yang formula the dual group
\left(2\pi\mathbb{Z}\right)^{3}
of the box
\Lambda
appears. It is however expected that there is a corresponding Lee-Huang-Yang term for gases with three-body interactions, which should appear in a third order expansion of the energy as a term of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}(1)
.

Proof strategy of Theorem
1
.
Following the ideas in
[
20
]
, respectively
[
2
,
3
,
11
,
12
]
, which have been developed in the context of Bose gases with two-body interactions, we are going to unveil the correlation structure of the ground state with the help of a suitable coordinate transformation. Based on the strategy presented in
[
8
]
, our initial coordinate transformation will be of algebraic nature, i.e. we introduce a new set of operators and observe that the many-body operator
H_{N}
is almost diagonal in these new variables. The algebraic approach immediately allows us to find satisfactory lower bounds on the ground state energy
E_{N}
. Furthermore, we show that this coordinate transformation can be realized in terms of a unitary map, at least in an approximate sense, which yields the corresponding upper bound on
E_{N}
.

In order to find a suitable transformation bringing
H_{N}
in a diagonal form, we observe that collisions between at most three particles will occur much more frequent compared to collisions between four or more particles, as we are in the dilute regime where the gas occupies only a volume of the magnitude
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
. Consequently, we first look for a diagonalization of a gas with only three particles
N=3
, which will involve the three-particle correlation structure
\omega
, and subsequently lift it to a diagonalization of the full many-body problem. As it turns out, including the three-particle correlation structure is enough to identify the leading order behaviour of the ground state energy. To be more precise, we are able to show at this point

\displaystyle E_{N}=\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right).

(14)

We want to emphasize that the proof of Eq. (
14
) depends on our ability to neglect collisions between four or more particles, and we note that the correlation structure involves mostly particles outside the state with zero-momentum. It is therefore crucial to have strong a priori information regarding the number of particles outside the state with zero momentum, which we will refer to as excited particles. In the language of second quantization, the number of excited particles can naturally be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}:=N-\mathcal{N}_{0}:=N-a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0},

(15)

where
N
is the total number of particles,
\mathcal{N}_{0}
counts the number of particles with zero momentum and
a_{0}
is the annihilation operator corresponding to the zero momentum state, see also Section
2
for a more comprehensive introduction. The following result, which has been verified in
[
20
]
, tells us that the number of excited particles is indeed small compared to the total number of particles
N
, i.e.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\braket{\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}}}=o_{N\rightarrow\infty}(1),

(16)

where
\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}}
is the ground state of the operator
H_{N}
. Using the a priori information in Eq. (
16
) then allows us to identify the leading order asymptotics of the ground state energy
E_{N}
in Eq. (
14
). In addition, we obtain at this point an improved (BEC) result

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\braket{\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}}}=O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)

(17)

with a rate of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)
, see also the subsequent Theorem
2
, which we believe to be of independent interest.

Finally, we use an additional coordinate transformation, which implements the two-particle and four-particle correlation structure
\xi
and
\eta
, together with the improved control on the number of excited particles in Eq. (
17
), in order to identify the coefficient
C
in front of the
\sqrt{N}
term in the energy asymptotics

\displaystyle C=\gamma(V)-\mu(V)-\sigma(V).

Notably, collisions between four particles do contribute to the subleading term in the energy expansion in Eq. (
13
), however in analogy to Eq. (
14
) we can dismiss collisions between five or more particles.

Theorem 2
.

Let
V
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
1
and let
\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}}
denote the ground state of the operator
H_{N}
. Furthermore let
\mathcal{N}
be the operator counting the number of excitations introduced in Eq. (
15
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\braket{\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}}}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}.

Outline.
In Subsection
1.1
we are first deriving the three-particle correlation structure for a model where the total number of particles is
N=3
. Following the strategy proposed in
[
8
]
, we are implementing in a systematic way the correlation structures from Subsection
1.1
for gases with many particles
N\gg 1
in Section
2
. Using Bose-Einstein condensation of the ground state as an input, this allows us to immediately recover the leading order behaviour of
E_{N}
as a lower bound and, in the subsequent Section
3
, also as an upper bound. Furthermore, we obtain at this point an improved version of (BEC) with a rate, which especially concludes the proof of Theorem
2
. In Section
4
, we are going to describe the two-particle and four-particle correlation structure, which gives rise to the correction
\mu(V)
and the correction
\sigma(V)
defined in Eq. (
8
) and Eq. (
12
) respectively. It is then the purpose of Subsection
4.2
to verify the lower bound in our main Theorem
1
, wherein we use the improved (BEC) result, and the purpose of Section
5
to verify the corresponding upper bound. The sign of
\gamma(\lambda V)-\mu(\lambda V)-\sigma(\lambda V)
is established in Section
6
for small
\lambda>0
, along side other useful properties of the scattering solutions that describe the correlation structure. Finally Appendix
A
contains a collection of operator inequalities, and Appendix
B
introduces a localization formula that allows us to improve (BEC) results, at the cost of modifying the state under consideration.

1.1
The three-body Problem

While one would naively expect that the leading order behaviour of the ground state energy
E_{N}
is dictated by the energy of the uncorrelated wave function
\Gamma_{0}(x_{1},\dots,x_{N}):=1

\displaystyle\braket{\Gamma_{0},H_{N}\Gamma_{0}}\!=\!\frac{N(N\!-\!1)(N\!-\!2)}{6N^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}V(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\!=\!\frac{N}{6}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}V(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\!+\!o_{N\rightarrow\infty}(N),

it is due to the presence of correlations in the ground state of the operator
H_{N}
, that the leading order coefficient
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
in the energy asymptotics of
E_{N}
in Eq. (
3
) satisfies

\displaystyle b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)<\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}V(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

In order to quantify the correlation energy
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}V(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y-b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
, we are going to follow the frame work developed in
[
8
]
, and investigate first the corresponding three-particle operator
H_{(3)}:=-\Delta_{3}+V_{N}
acting on
L^{2}(\Lambda^{3})
, where
\Delta_{3}:=\Delta_{x_{1}}+\Delta_{x_{2}}+\Delta_{x_{3}}
, before we study the many particle operator
H_{N}
defined in Eq. (
2
). It will be our goal to find a transformation

\displaystyle T:L^{2}(\Lambda^{3})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\Lambda^{3})

that removes correlations between states with low momenta and states with high momenta, i.e. we want to bring
H_{(3)}
in a block-diagonal form, which allows us to extract the correlation energy. In is content of Section
2
to lift the block-diagonalization from the three-particle problem, described by the transformation
T
, to a block-diagonalization of the many-particle operator
H_{N}
, which will allow us to identify the correlation energy for the many-particle problem.

Let us first specify the set of low momenta as either the set where all three particles occupy the zero momentum state

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}:=\{(0,0,0)\}\subseteq(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{9}

(18)

or the set where at most one of the three particles is allowed to have non-zero momentum

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{K}:=\bigcup_{|k|\leq K}\{(k,0,0),(0,k,0),(0,0,k)\}\subseteq(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{9},

where
0\leq K<\infty
is a parameter that we are going to specify later. For the purpose of extracting the correlation energy, it is enough to consider
K:=0
, however for technical reasons it is going to be convenient later to consider positive values
K>0
as well. Having the set
\mathcal{L}_{K}
at hand, we can define the projection
\pi_{K}
onto states with low momenta as

\displaystyle\pi_{K}(\Psi):=\sum_{(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3})\in\mathcal{L}_{K}}\braket{u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}},\Psi}u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}},

(19)

where
u_{k}(x):=e^{ikx}
for
k\in(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{3}
and
u_{k_{1}}u_{k_{2}}u_{k_{3}}
has to be understood as the function
u_{k_{1}}(x_{1})u_{k_{2}}(x_{2})u_{k_{3}}(x_{3})
. Let us furthermore introduce the projection
Q
acting on
L^{2}(\Lambda)
as

\displaystyle Q(\phi):=\sum_{k\neq 0}\braket{u_{k},\phi}u_{k}.

Following the strategy in
[
8
]
, let
R
be the pseudo-inverse of the operator
Q^{\otimes 3}(-\Delta_{3}+V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}
and let us define the Feshbach-Schur like transformation

\displaystyle T:=1+RV_{N}\pi_{K}.

(20)

Note that
T
would be a proper Feshbach-Schur map, in case we would exchange
Q^{\otimes 3}
with the projection
1-\pi_{K}
, however we prefer to work with
Q^{\otimes 3}
for technical reasons. Conjugating
H_{(3)}
with
T^{-1}=1-RV_{N}\pi_{K}
then yields the approximate block-diagonalization

\displaystyle(T^{-1})^{\dagger}H_{(3)}T^{-1}=-\Delta_{3}+\pi_{K}\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)\pi_{K}+\!(1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left\{\pi_{K}\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)\left(1-\pi_{K}-Q^{\otimes 3}\right)+\mathrm{H.c.}\right\},

where we introduced the notation
\{A+\mathrm{H.c.}\}:=A+A^{*}
and made use of the identity

\displaystyle H_{(3)}T^{-1}

\displaystyle=H_{(3)}-H_{(3)}RV_{N}\pi_{K}=H_{(3)}-Q^{\otimes 3}H_{(3)}RV_{N}\pi_{K}-(1-Q^{\otimes 3})H_{(3)}RV_{N}\pi_{K}

\displaystyle=H_{(3)}-Q^{\otimes 3}V_{N}\pi_{K}-(1-Q^{\otimes 3})V_{N}RV_{N}\pi_{K}.

Defining the almost block-diagonal renormalized potential
\widetilde{V}_{N}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{N}\!:=\pi_{K}\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)\pi_{K}+\!(1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\left\{\left(1-\pi_{K}-Q^{\otimes 3}\right)\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)\pi_{K}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right\},

(21)

we therefore obtain the algebraic identity

\displaystyle H_{(3)}=T^{\dagger}\left(-\Delta_{3}+\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)T.

(22)

The presence of
\left\{\left(1-\pi_{K}-Q^{\otimes 3}\right)\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)\pi_{K}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right\}
in
\widetilde{V}_{N}
, which are the terms that violate the block-diagonal structure, is due to the usage of
Q^{\otimes 3}
instead of
1-\pi_{K}
, however it turns out that these terms do not contribute to the correlation energy to leading order. One therefore expects to read of the leading order coefficient
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
in the asymptotic expansion of the ground state energy
E_{N}
in Eq. (
3
) from the matrix entries of the renormalized potential

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)_{000,000}=\braket{u_{0}u_{0}u_{0},\widetilde{V}_{N}u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}}=\braket{u_{0}u_{0}u_{0},\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}}.

As we are going to verify in Lemma
16
, we have indeed the asymptotic result

\displaystyle b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)=N^{2}\braket{u_{0}u_{0}u_{0},\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}}+O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

2
First Order Lower Bound

It is the goal of this Section to bring, in analogy to Eq. (
22
), the many-particle operator
H_{N}
in an approximate block-diagonal form, which allows us to obtain an asymptotically correct lower bound on the ground state energy
E_{N}
in Corollary
1
. For this purpose let us first rewrite the operator
H_{N}
defined in Eq. (
2
) in the language of second quantization as

\displaystyle H_{N}=\sum_{k\in(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{3}}|k|^{2}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn\in(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{3}}\left(V_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n},

(23)

where
a_{k}:=a(u_{k})
annihilates a particle in the mode
u_{k}(x):=e^{ik\cdot x}
and its adjoint
a_{k}^{\dagger}
creates a particle in the mode
u_{k}
, see e.g.
[
9
]
for an introduction to the standard creation and annihilation operators, and
\left(V_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}
are the matrix elements of
V_{N}
w.r.t. to the basis
u_{i}u_{j}u_{k}
defined below Eq. (
19
). If not indicated otherwise, we will always assume that indices run in the set
(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{3}
, which we will usually neglect in our notation, and we write
k\neq 0
in case the index runs in the set
(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{3}\setminus\{0\}
. Note that the operator on the right hand side of Eq. (
23
) is defined on the full Fock space

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\!\left(L^{2}\!\left(\Lambda\right)\right):=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}L^{2}_{\mathrm{sym}}\!\left(\Lambda^{n}\right),

while the left hand side is only defined on
L^{2}_{\mathrm{sym}}\!\left(\Lambda^{N}\right)\subseteq\mathcal{F}\!\left(L^{2}\!\left(\Lambda\right)\right)
, and therefore Eq. (
23
) has to be understood as being restricted to the subspace
L^{2}_{\mathrm{sym}}\!\left(\Lambda^{N}\right)
. Furthermore, we observe that
V_{N}
is a translation-invariant multiplication operator, and therefore the matrix elements of
V_{N}
satisfy
\left(V_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}=0
in case
i+j+k\neq\ell+m+n
and otherwise

\displaystyle\left(V_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}=\left(V_{N}\right)_{(i-\ell)(j-m)(k-n),000}=N^{-2}\widehat{V}\!\left(\frac{j-m}{\sqrt{N}},\frac{k-n}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

(24)

Following the strategy proposed in
[
8
]
, we are going to introduce a many-particle counterpart to the three particle map
T
defined in Eq. (
20
), which is realized by the set of operators

\displaystyle c_{k}:

\displaystyle=a_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij,\ell mn}(T-1)_{ijk,\ell mn}\,a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n},

(25)

\displaystyle\psi_{ijk}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell mn}T_{ijk,\ell mn}\,a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}.

(26)

Here
T_{ijk,\ell mn}:=\braket{u_{i}u_{j}u_{k},Tu_{\ell}u_{m}u_{n}}
denotes the matrix elements of
T
. The following Lemma
1
is the many-particle counterpart to Eq. (
22
), in the sense that it provides an (approximate) block-diagonal representation of the operator
H_{N}
in terms of the new variables
c_{k}
and
\psi_{ijk}
.

Lemma 1
.

Let
\widetilde{V}_{N}
be the operator defined in Eq. (
21
). Then we have

\displaystyle H_{N}=\sum_{k}|k|^{2}

\displaystyle c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\!+\!\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\psi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\psi_{\ell mn}-\mathcal{E},

(27)

where the residual term
\mathcal{E}
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}:=\frac{1}{4}

\displaystyle\sum_{ijk,\ell mn;i^{\prime}j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}m^{\prime}}|k|^{2}\overline{(T-1)_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k,\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}n^{\prime}}}(T-1)_{ijk,\ell mn}\,a_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{n^{\prime}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\times\left(a_{i^{\prime}}a_{j^{\prime}}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}-\delta_{ii^{\prime}}\delta_{jj^{\prime}}-\delta_{ij^{\prime}}\delta_{ji^{\prime}}\right)a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}.

Proof.

Using the permutation symmetry of
T
, we first identify
\sum_{k}|k|^{2}(c_{k}-a_{k})^{\dagger}(c_{k}-a_{k})
as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}

\displaystyle\sum_{ijk,\ell mn;i^{\prime}j^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}m^{\prime}}|k|^{2}\overline{(T-1)_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k,\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}n^{\prime}}}(T-1)_{ijk,\ell mn}\,a_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{n^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{i^{\prime}}a_{j^{\prime}}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\!\!\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left\{(T-1)^{\dagger}(-\Delta_{x_{3}})(T-1)\right\}_{ijk,\ell mn}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}+\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\!\!\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left\{(T-1)^{\dagger}(-\Delta_{3})(T-1)\right\}_{ijk,\ell mn}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}+\mathcal{E},

where
\Delta_{3}
is the Laplace operator on
L^{2}(\Lambda)^{\otimes 3}
. Similarly

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2}a_{k}^{\dagger}(c_{k}-a_{k})+\mathrm{H.c.}=\frac{1}{6}\!\!\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\Big\{(-\Delta_{3})(T-1)+\mathrm{H.c.}\Big\}_{ijk,\ell mn}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n},

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\psi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\psi_{\ell mn}=\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(T^{\dagger}\widetilde{V}_{N}T\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}a^{\dagger}_{i}a^{\dagger}_{j}a^{\dagger}_{k}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}.

Since

\displaystyle(T\!-\!1)^{\dagger}\Delta_{3}(T\!-\!1)\!+\!\left\{\Delta_{3}(T\!-\!1)\!+\!\mathrm{H.c.}\right\}\!=\!T^{\dagger}\Delta_{3}T\!-\!\Delta_{3}

we obtain

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\!+\!\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\psi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\psi_{\ell mn}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\!+\!\frac{1}{6}\!\!\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left\{T^{\dagger}\left(-\Delta_{3}+\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)T\!+\!\Delta_{3}\right\}_{ijk,\ell mn}\!\!a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}\!+\!\mathcal{E}.

We observe that
T^{\dagger}\left(-\Delta_{3}+\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)T\!+\!\Delta_{3}=V_{N}
by Eq. (
22
), which concludes the proof by the representation of
H_{N}
in second quantization, see Eq. (
23
).
∎

Making use of the sign
(1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})\geq 0
, we immediately obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left((1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\psi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\psi_{\ell mn}\geq 0.

Therefore Lemma
1
allows us to bound
H_{N}
from below by

\displaystyle H_{N}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}-(1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\psi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\psi_{\ell mn}-\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}-(1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}-\mathcal{E},

(28)

where we have used the fact that
\psi_{ijk}=a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}
in case one of the indices is zero, which is a direct consequence of the observation that
(T-1)_{ijk,\ell mn}=0
in case one of the indices in
\{i,j,k\}
is zero. Note that we can write

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{N}-(1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})=A+B+B^{*}

with
A
and
B
defined as

\displaystyle A:

\displaystyle=\pi_{K}\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)\pi_{K},

\displaystyle B:

\displaystyle=\left(1-\pi_{K}-Q^{\otimes 3}\right)\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)\pi_{K}.

Let us first analyse the term involving
A

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(A\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}=\lambda_{0,0}\,(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}+9a_{0}^{2\dagger}a_{0}^{2}\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}\lambda_{k,0}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k},

(29)

where we define the coefficients

\displaystyle\lambda_{k,\ell}:=\frac{1}{18}\braket{u_{0}u_{\ell}u_{k-\ell},(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N})(u_{0}u_{0}u_{k}+u_{0}u_{k}u_{0}+u_{k}u_{0}u_{0})}.

To keep the notation light, we do not explicitly indicate the
N
dependence of
\lambda_{k,\ell}
. Similarly

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(B\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}=3a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\sum_{\ell\neq 0}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}+9a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}\sum_{\ell,0<|k|\leq K}\lambda_{k,\ell}a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k}.

(30)

Putting together Eq. (
28
), Eq. (
29
) and Eq. (
30
) yields

\displaystyle H_{N}\geq\lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}+\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}+\mathcal{E}^{\prime}-\mathcal{E},

(31)

where we define the operator
\mathbb{Q}_{K}
and the error term
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{K}:

\displaystyle=9a_{0}^{2\dagger}a_{0}^{2}\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}\lambda_{k,0}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}+3\left(a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\sum_{0<|\ell|\leq K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right),

(32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime}:

\displaystyle=\bigg(3\!\sum_{|\ell|>K}\!\!\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\!+\!9\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\ell,0<|k|\leq K}\!\!\lambda_{k,\ell}a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}\!+\!\mathrm{H.c.}\bigg).

(33)

The following Lemma
2
, Lemma
3
and Lemma
4
will give us sufficient bounds on the various terms appearing in Eq. (
31
), in order to establish that the ground state energy
E_{N}
of
H_{N}
is, to leading order, bounded from below by
\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N
, see Corollary
1
. In our first Lemma
2
we provide a lower bound on

\displaystyle\lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}

for
K
large enough, which is an operator that is at most quadratic in the variables
a_{k}
and
a_{k}^{\dagger}
for
k\neq 0
. In the following let us denote with

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}:=\sum_{k\neq 0}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}

the operator that counts the number of excited particles, i.e. the number of particles with momentum
k\neq 0
. Since we have the operator identity
\sum_{k}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}=N
on the Hilbert space
L^{2}_{\mathrm{sym}}(\Lambda^{N})\subseteq\mathcal{F}\!\left(L^{2}\!\left(\Lambda\right)\right)
, we observe that
a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}=N-\mathcal{N}
, see also Eq. (
15
), i.e. the number of particles with momentum
k=0
is given by the difference between the total number of particles
N
and the number of excited particles.

Lemma 2
.

Let
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
be the modified scattering length defined in Eq. (
4
). Then there exists for all
\tau,\alpha>0
constants
C,K>0
such that

\displaystyle\lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N-\alpha\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}-C\left(1+\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N}+\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

Proof.

First of all we observe that we can write

\displaystyle(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}

\displaystyle=N^{3}-3N^{2}(\mathcal{N}+3)+N(3\mathcal{N}^{2}+6\mathcal{N}+2)-\mathcal{N}^{3}-3\mathcal{N}^{2}-2\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\geq N^{3}-3N^{2}\mathcal{N}-N^{2}C,

for a suitable
C>0
. Defining

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{>}:=\mathcal{N}-\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k},

we therefore we obtain in combination with Lemma
16

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}-\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)\left\{9\frac{(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}+3\left(\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}}{N^{2}}\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{-k}^{\dagger}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)-3\mathcal{N}-CN^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}\right\}-C

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)\bigg\{\left(9\frac{(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{2}}-3-CN^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +3\left(\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}}{N^{2}}\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{-k}^{\dagger}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)-3\mathcal{N}_{>}\bigg\}-C,

for a suitable constant
C>0
. Since

\displaystyle 3\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}}{N^{2}}\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{-k}^{\dagger}+\mathrm{H.c.}\leq 6\frac{(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k},

and since we have

\displaystyle\left(3\frac{(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{2}}-3-CN^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\sum_{0<|k|\leq K}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\geq-3\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N}-C\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}},

we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N-\frac{1}{2}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)\mathcal{N}_{>}-C\left(1+\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N}+\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)

for a suitable constant
C
. Using
\mathcal{N}_{>}\leq K^{-2\tau}\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}
, this concludes the proof for
K
large enough.
∎

In the subsequent Lemma
3
, we provide estimates on the residual term
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
1
, which will allow us to compare the size of
\mathcal{E}
with the kinetic energy
\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}
in the variables
c_{k}
.

Lemma 3
.

For
K\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C_{K}>0
, such that

\displaystyle\pm\mathcal{E}\leq C_{K}\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)c_{k}+\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{3}}\right)\!(\mathcal{N}+1).

Proof.

Let us denote with
\mathcal{I}\subseteq(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{3\times 3}
the index set

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}:=\{(0,0,0)\}\cup\bigcup_{0<|\ell|\leq K}\{(\ell,0,0),(0,\ell,0),(0,0,\ell)\}

Then we define for
I=(I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}),I^{\prime}=(I^{\prime}_{1},I^{\prime}_{2},I^{\prime}_{3})\in\mathcal{I}
the operator
K^{(I,I^{\prime})}
acting on
L^{2}(\Lambda)
and the operator
G^{(I,I^{\prime})}
acting on
L^{2}(\Lambda)^{\otimes 2}
as

\displaystyle K^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{i,i^{\prime}}:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{jk}|k|^{2}\overline{(T-1)_{i^{\prime}jk,I^{\prime}}}\left((T-1)_{ijk,I}+(T-1)_{jik,I}\right),

\displaystyle G^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k}|k|^{2}\overline{(T-1)_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k,I^{\prime}}}(T-1)_{ijk,I},

as well as
\mathcal{K}_{\tau,2}:=(-\Delta_{x_{1}})^{\tau}+(-\Delta_{x_{2}})^{\tau}
acting on
L^{2}(\Lambda)^{\otimes 2}
. Then we can write
\mathcal{E}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=\sum_{I,I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}}\left(\sum_{i,i^{\prime}}K^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{i,i^{\prime}}a^{\dagger}_{i}\left(a_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{I_{3}}^{\dagger}a_{I^{\prime}_{1}}a_{I^{\prime}_{2}}a_{I^{\prime}_{3}}\right)a_{i^{\prime}}+\sum_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}G^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}a^{\dagger}_{i}a_{j}^{\dagger}\left(a_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{I_{3}}^{\dagger}a_{I^{\prime}_{1}}a_{I^{\prime}_{2}}a_{I^{\prime}_{3}}\right)a_{i^{\prime}}a_{j^{\prime}}\right).

(34)

By the weighted Schur test, the operator norm of
\mathcal{K}_{\tau,2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G^{(I,I^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}_{\tau,2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{K}_{\tau,2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G^{(I,I^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}_{\tau,2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\leq\sqrt{\alpha^{(I,I^{\prime})}\alpha^{(I^{\prime},I)}},

where we define
\alpha^{(I,I^{\prime})}:=\sup_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\sum_{ij}\frac{|G^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}|}{|i|^{2\tau}+|j|^{2\tau}}
. Let us furthermore introduce
s:=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}
and
s^{\prime}:=I^{\prime}_{1}+I^{\prime}_{2}+I^{\prime}_{3}
. Making use of Lemma
15
, we obtain for the concrete choice
\tau:=\frac{2}{3}

\displaystyle\alpha^{(I,I^{\prime})}\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\sup_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\!\sum_{ijk}\!\frac{|k|^{2}|(T\!-\!1)_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k,I^{\prime}}|\,|(T\!-\!1)_{ijk,I}|}{|i|^{2\tau}\!+\!|j|^{2\tau}}\!\lesssim\!N^{-4}\sup_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\!\sum_{ijk\neq 0}\frac{\delta_{i^{\prime}+j^{\prime}+k=s^{\prime}}\delta_{i+j+k=s}}{(|i|^{2\tau}\!+\!|j|^{2\tau})(|i|^{2}\!+\!|j|^{2}\!+\!|k|^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\!N^{-4}\!\sum_{i\neq 0}\frac{1}{|i|^{2+2\tau}}\lesssim N^{-4}.

Consequently
\|\mathcal{K}_{\tau,2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G^{(I,I^{\prime})}\mathcal{K}_{\tau,2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\lesssim N^{-4}
. Furthermore, the operator

\displaystyle X^{(I,I^{\prime})}:=a_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{I_{3}}^{\dagger}a_{I^{\prime}_{1}}a_{I^{\prime}_{2}}a_{I^{\prime}_{3}}

satisfies
\|X^{(I,I^{\prime})}\|\leq N^{3}
. Therefore we obtain by Corollary
7

\displaystyle\sum_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}G^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}a^{\dagger}_{i}a_{j}^{\dagger}\left(a_{I_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{I_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{I_{3}}^{\dagger}a_{I^{\prime}_{1}}a_{I^{\prime}_{2}}a_{I^{\prime}_{3}}\right)a_{i^{\prime}}a_{j^{\prime}}\lesssim\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}c_{k}+(\mathcal{N}+N^{\tau})\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}.

Again by Lemma
15
we have
\|K^{(I,I^{\prime})}\|\lesssim N^{-\frac{7}{2}}
, which concludes the proof by Corollary
7
, together with the observation that the set
\mathcal{I}
in the definition of
\mathcal{E}
in Eq. (
34
) is finite.
∎

The next Lemma
4
will give us sufficient bounds on the error term
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
defined in Eq. (
33
), which will be responsible for the appearance of an order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
error in the main results of this Section Theorem
3
and Corollary
1
.

Lemma 4
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for
K\leq\sqrt{N}
, where
K
is as in the definition of
\pi_{K}
below Eq. (
19
), and
\epsilon>0

\displaystyle\pm\left(\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\!+\!\mathrm{H.c.}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\sum_{\ell}|\ell|^{2}c_{\ell}^{\dagger}c_{\ell}\!+\!\epsilon\,\mathcal{N}\!+\!C\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}\!+\!\frac{C}{\epsilon}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\!+\!\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{K\!+\!1}}\right),

(35)

\displaystyle\pm\left(\sum_{\ell}\lambda_{k,\ell}a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\sum_{\ell}|\ell|^{2}c_{\ell}^{\dagger}c_{\ell}+\epsilon\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N}+C\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}+\frac{C}{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N}\right).

(36)

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\pm\left(\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}\!+\!\mathrm{H.c.}\right)\leq N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{\ell}|\ell|^{2}c_{\ell}^{\dagger}c_{\ell}+\mathcal{N}\right)+N^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{N}^{2}\left(\mathcal{N}+\sqrt{N}\right).

(37)

Proof.

Given
m\in\{0,1\}
, let us define the operator
X:=a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\frac{\mathcal{N}^{m}}{N^{m}}
and the coefficients

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(n)}_{\ell,k}:=\overline{(T-1)_{(n-k)(k-\ell)\ell,n00}}+\overline{(T-1)_{(n-k)(k-\ell)\ell,0n0}}+\overline{(T-1)_{(n-k)(k-\ell)\ell,00n}},

and observe that by Lemma
15
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|\Lambda^{(n)}_{\ell,k}|\leq\frac{C}{N^{2}(|\ell|^{2}+|k|^{2})}\left(1+\frac{|\ell|^{2}+|k|^{2}}{N}\right)^{-1},

(38)

where we have assumed w.l.o.g. that
|n|\leq\sqrt{N}
, since
\Lambda^{(n)}_{\ell,k}=0
in case
|n|>K
and
K\leq\sqrt{N}
. In order to verify Eq. (
35
), respectively Eq. (
37
), let us write

\displaystyle\ \ \ \sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\frac{\mathcal{N}^{m}}{N^{m}}=\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,c_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}X-\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}X

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,c_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}X-\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger}X-\sum_{|\ell|>K}\sum_{n\neq 0}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,\Lambda^{(n)}_{\ell,0}a_{0}^{2\dagger}a_{n}^{\dagger}a_{n}X.

(39)

Regarding the first term in in Eq. (
39
), note that we have for
\epsilon>0
the estimate

\displaystyle\pm\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,c_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}X\pm\mathrm{H.c.}\leq\epsilon\sum_{\ell}|\ell|^{2}c_{\ell}^{\dagger}c_{\ell}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}X^{\dagger}\left(\sum_{|\ell|>K}\frac{|\lambda_{0,\ell}|^{2}}{\ell^{2}}a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}+\sum_{|\ell|>K}\frac{|\lambda_{0,\ell}|^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)X.

Using
|\lambda_{k,\ell}|\lesssim N^{-2}(1+\frac{|\ell|^{2}}{N})^{-1}
, see Lemma
15
, we have
\sum_{|\ell|>K}\frac{|\lambda_{0,\ell}|^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\lesssim N^{-\frac{7}{2}}
and
\frac{|\lambda_{0,\ell}|^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{N^{4}(K^{2}+1)}
for
|\ell|>K
, and therefore we obtain for such
K

\displaystyle\pm\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,c_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}X

\displaystyle\pm\mathrm{H.c.}\lesssim\epsilon\sum_{\ell}|\ell|^{2}c_{\ell}^{\dagger}c_{\ell}\!+\!\frac{1}{\epsilon N^{4}}X^{\dagger}\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{K^{2}+1}\!+\!\sqrt{N}\right)X

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\sum_{\ell}|\ell|^{2}c_{\ell}^{\dagger}c_{\ell}\!+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2m}}{N^{2m}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{K^{2}+1}\!+\!\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\epsilon}\right),

where we have used
X^{\dagger}X\lesssim N^{2}\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2m}}{N^{2m}}
and
[X,\mathcal{N}]=0
. Regarding the second term in Eq. (
39
), let us use
\sum_{|\ell|>K}|\ell|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|N^{4}\lambda_{0,\ell}|^{2}a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{K+1}}
as well as the fact that
\sum_{\ell}\sqrt{|\ell|}(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger}\lesssim\mathcal{N}
by Lemma
20
, to estimate for
\kappa>0

\displaystyle\pm\!\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}(c_{\ell}\!-\!a_{\ell})^{\dagger}X\pm\mathrm{H.c.}\lesssim\frac{1}{\kappa\sqrt{K+1}}\mathcal{N}+\frac{\kappa}{N^{4}}X^{\dagger}\mathcal{N}X\leq\frac{1}{\kappa\sqrt{K+1}}\mathcal{N}+\kappa\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2m}}{N^{2m}}\mathcal{N}.

Regarding the final term in Eq. (
39
) we have that
\sum_{\ell}|\Lambda^{(n)}_{\ell,0}|\lesssim N^{-\frac{3}{2}}
by Eq. (
38
), and hence

\displaystyle\pm\sum_{|\ell|>K}\sum_{n\neq 0}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,\Lambda^{(n)}_{\ell,0}a_{0}^{2\dagger}a_{n}^{\dagger}a_{n}X\pm\mathrm{H.c.}\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}.

For
m=0
, the choice
\kappa:=\epsilon
yields Eq. (
35
) and for
m=1
the choice
\kappa:=\sqrt{N}
and
\epsilon:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
yields Eq. (
37
).

Regarding the proof of Eq. (
36
), let us define the operators
d_{\ell}:=\lambda_{k,\ell}N^{\frac{3}{2}}a_{k-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k}
and write
a^{\dagger}_{\ell}d_{\ell}=c_{\ell}^{\dagger}d_{\ell}+d_{\ell}(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger}+[(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger},d_{\ell}]
. We compute

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}

\displaystyle[(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger},d_{\ell}]=\frac{1}{2}N^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big(a^{3\dagger}_{0}\sum_{ij\ell}\frac{1}{3}\Lambda^{(0)}_{\ell,-i}\lambda_{k,\ell}[a_{i}a_{-i-\ell},a_{k-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k}]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +a^{2\dagger}_{0}\sum_{|n|\leq K}\sum_{i\ell}\Lambda^{(n)}_{\ell,n-i}\lambda_{k,\ell}[a^{\dagger}_{n}a_{i}a_{n-i-\ell},a_{k-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k}]\Big)\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{0}^{3\dagger}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mu^{(1)}_{k}a_{k}a_{-k}\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{a_{0}^{2\dagger}}{N}\sum_{|n|\leq K}\mu^{(2)}_{k,n}a^{\dagger}_{n}a_{k}a_{n-k}\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}-\frac{a_{0}^{2\dagger}}{N}\sum_{i,\ell}\mu^{(3)}_{k,i,\ell}a^{\dagger}_{k-\ell}a_{i}a_{k-i-\ell}\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}},

where we define the coefficients

\displaystyle\mu^{(1)}_{k}:

\displaystyle=N^{3}\sum_{\ell}\frac{1}{3}\Lambda^{(0)}_{\ell,k}\lambda_{k,\ell},

\displaystyle\mu^{(2)}_{k,n}:

\displaystyle=N^{\frac{5}{2}}\sum_{\ell}\Lambda^{(n)}_{\ell,k}\lambda_{k,\ell},

\displaystyle\mu^{(3)}_{k,i,\ell}:

\displaystyle=N^{\frac{5}{2}}\Lambda^{(k)}_{\ell,k-i}\lambda_{k,\ell}.

Using again Eq. (
38
) and
|\lambda_{k,\ell}|\lesssim N^{-2}(1+\frac{|\ell|^{2}}{N})^{-1}
, we immediately obtain
|\mu^{(1)}_{k}|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
,
|\mu^{(2)}_{k,n}|\lesssim\frac{1}{N}
,
|\mu^{(3)}_{k,i,\ell}|\lesssim N^{-\frac{3}{2}}
and
\sum_{i}|\mu^{(3)}_{k,i,\ell}|\lesssim\frac{1}{N}
, and therefore by Cauchy-Schwarz

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}\left([(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger},d_{\ell}]\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}.

Consequently

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\ell}a_{\ell}^{\dagger}d_{\ell}\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)\lesssim\epsilon\sum_{\ell}|\ell|^{2}c_{\ell}^{\dagger}c_{\ell}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\sum_{\ell}\frac{1}{|\ell|^{2}}d_{\ell}^{\dagger}d_{\ell}+\epsilon\sum_{\ell}d_{\ell}d_{\ell}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{\epsilon}\sum_{\ell}\frac{a_{0}^{3\dagger}a_{0}}{N^{2}}(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger}\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}}{N^{2}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}.

Similar to the proof of Eq. (
35
), we observe that
\sum_{\ell}d_{\ell}d_{\ell}^{\dagger}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}\mathcal{N}
and, using Lemma
20
,

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \sum_{\ell}\frac{a_{0}^{3\dagger}a_{0}}{N^{2}}(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})(c_{\ell}-a_{\ell})^{\dagger}\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}}{N^{2}}\lesssim\frac{a_{0}^{3\dagger}a_{0}}{N^{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N}\frac{a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}}{N^{2}}\leq\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N},

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}\frac{1}{|\ell|^{2}}d_{\ell}^{\dagger}d_{\ell}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}+N^{3}\sum_{\ell}\frac{1}{|\ell|^{2}}|\lambda_{k,\ell}|^{2}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k-\ell}a_{k}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N}.

∎

Having Lemma
2
, Lemma
3
and Lemma
4
at hand, we can use the lower bound in Eq. (
31
) in order to derive the following Theorem
3
, which provides strong lower bounds on the quantity
\braket{\Psi,H_{N}\Psi}
. Note however that Theorem
3
is only applicable for states
\Psi
satisfying (BEC) in the spectral sense
\mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\leq\epsilon N\right)\Psi=\Psi
, where the orthogonal projection
\mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\leq\epsilon N\right)
is defined by the means of functional calculus. Here we refer to a Hilbert space element
\Psi
as a state, in case
\|\Psi\|=1
.

Theorem 3
.

There exist constants
\delta,C>0
and
\epsilon>0
, such that

\displaystyle\braket{\Psi,H_{N}\Psi}\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+\delta\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+\delta\braket{\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi}-C\sqrt{N}

for any state
\Psi
satisfying
\mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\leq\epsilon N\right)\Psi=\Psi
, where
\mathcal{N}:=\sum_{k\neq 0}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}
.

Proof.

By Eq. (
31
) together with the estimates in Lemma
2
, Lemma
3
and Lemma
4
we can bound for
\alpha,\tau>0
the operator
H_{N}-\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N
from below by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}-C\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)c_{k}-\alpha\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}-C\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\mathcal{N}-CN^{\frac{1}{2}},

for a suitable constant
C
. In the following let
\Psi
be a state satisfying
\mathds{1}_{[0,\epsilon N)}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\right)\Psi=\Psi
and define
\Psi_{k}:=c_{k}\Psi
. By the definition of
c_{k}
it is clear that
\mathds{1}_{[0,\epsilon N+2)}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\right)\Psi_{k}=\Psi_{k}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\ \ \left\langle\Psi,\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)c_{k}\Psi\right\rangle=\sum_{k}|k|^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{k},\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\Psi_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}|k|^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{k},\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\mathds{1}_{[0,\epsilon N+2)}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\right)\Psi_{k}\right\rangle\leq\sum_{k}|k|^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{k},\left(\frac{\epsilon N+2}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\Psi_{k}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\epsilon N+2}{N}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left\langle\Psi,\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Psi\right\rangle.

In a similar fashion we have
\braket{\Psi,\mathcal{N}^{2}\Psi}\leq\epsilon N\braket{\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi}
. Furthermore, note that

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\lesssim\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+\frac{\mathcal{N}^{2}}{N}+N^{\frac{1}{2}}

by Lemma
20
for
\tau<\frac{1}{2}
. Choosing
\alpha
small enough, we therefore obtain

\displaystyle\braket{\Psi,H_{N}\Psi}\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+\frac{1}{3}\left\langle\Psi,\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Psi\right\rangle-CN^{\frac{1}{2}}-\epsilon C\braket{\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi}

for states
\Psi
satisfying
\mathds{1}_{[0,\epsilon N)}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\right)\Psi=\Psi
. Again by Lemma
20
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(1-R\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}\right)\leq R\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+R

for a suitable constant
R
. Using
\mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\leq\epsilon N\right)\Psi=\Psi
with
\epsilon
small enough such that
R\epsilon<1
, we therefore have
\braket{\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi}\lesssim\left\langle\Psi,\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Psi\right\rangle+1
. Choosing
\epsilon
small enough concludes the proof.
∎

Before we come to the lower bound on the ground state energy
E_{N}
in the main result of this Section Corollary
1
, let us first state the corresponding upper bound in the subsequent Theorem
4
. The proof of Theorem
4
is content of the following Section
3
.

Theorem 4
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that the ground state energy
E_{N}
is bounded from above by
E_{N}\leq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+C\sqrt{N}
.

It has been verified in
[
20
]
, for the more general setting of particles being confined by an additional external potential, that any approximate ground state
\Psi_{N}
of the operator
H_{N}
satisfies complete Bose-Einstein condensation
\braket{\Psi_{N},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{N}}=o_{N\rightarrow\infty}(N)
. Combining this observation with Theorem
3
, allows us to derive an asymptotically correct lower bound on the ground state energy in Corollary
1
with an error of the order
\sqrt{N}
, see Eq. (
42
). In this context we call
\Psi_{N}
an approximate ground state, in case
\|\Psi_{N}\|=1
and there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\braket{\Psi_{N},H_{N}\Psi_{N}}\leq E_{N}+C.

(40)

Note that the assumption in Eq. (
40
) is more restrictive compared to the one employed in
[
20
]
, where the authors call
\Psi_{N}
an approximate ground state in case
\|\Psi_{N}\|=1
and

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\braket{\Psi_{N},H_{N}\Psi_{N}}=\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V).

(41)

The fact that Eq. (
40
) implies Eq. (
41
) follows immediately from the leading order asymptotics in Eq. (
3
), which has been verified in
[
20
]
, together with the trivial lower bound
\braket{\Psi_{N},H_{N}\Psi_{N}}\geq E_{N}
. Furthermore, in combination with the upper bound on
E_{N}
derived in Theorem
4
, we obtain that the ground state
\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}}
of
H_{N}
satisfies (BEC) with a rate
\frac{\sqrt{N}}{N}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
, which concludes the proof of our second main Theorem
2
. Finally we can improve this result to (BEC) in the spectral sense

\displaystyle\mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\leq K\sqrt{N}\right)\Phi_{N}=\Phi_{N},

however we have to consider slightly modified states
\Phi_{N}
here.

Corollary 1
.

The ground state
\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}}
of the operator
H_{N}
satisfies for a suitable
C>0

\displaystyle\braket{\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{N}^{\mathrm{GS}}}\leq C\sqrt{N},

and we have the lower bound

\displaystyle E_{N}\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N-C\sqrt{N}.

(42)

Furthermore there exists a constant
C>0
and states
\Phi_{N}
, such that
\Phi_{N}
is an approximate ground state of
H_{N}
satisfying (BEC) in the spectral sense with rate
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\braket{\Phi_{N},H_{N}\Phi_{N}}\leq E_{N}+C,

\displaystyle\mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\leq C\sqrt{N}\right)\Phi_{N}=\Phi_{N},

and we have the estimate on the kinetic energy
\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle\leq C\sqrt{N}
.

Proof.

From the results in
[
20
]
we know that the ground state
\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N}
of
H_{N}
satisfies

\displaystyle\braket{\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N},\mathcal{N}\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N}}=o_{N\rightarrow\infty}(N).

Consequently we know by Lemma
21
that there exist states
\xi_{N}
satisfying

\displaystyle\braket{\xi_{N},H_{N}\xi_{N}}\leq E_{N}+C\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon\sqrt{N}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}N}\right)

(43)

and
\mathds{1}(\mathcal{N}\leq\epsilon N)\xi_{N}=\xi_{N}
, where we choose
\epsilon>0
as in Theorem
3
. By Theorem
3
and Theorem
4
we therefore obtain for a suitable constant
C>0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+\delta\left\langle\xi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\xi_{N}\right\rangle+\delta\braket{\xi_{N},\mathcal{N}\xi_{N}}-C\sqrt{N}\leq\braket{\xi_{N},H_{N}\xi_{N}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq E_{N}+C\leq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+C\sqrt{N}.

(44)

This immediately implies
E_{N}\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N-C\sqrt{N}
for a suitable constant
C>0
and

\displaystyle\braket{\xi_{N},\mathcal{N}\xi_{N}}=O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right),

(45)

as well as
\left\langle\xi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\xi_{N}\right\rangle=O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
. Furthermore, there exist by Lemma
21
states
\widetilde{\xi}_{N}
satisfying
\mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}>\frac{\epsilon}{2}N\right)\widetilde{\xi}_{N}=\widetilde{\xi}_{N}
and

\displaystyle\braket{\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N},\mathcal{N}\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N}}\lesssim\braket{\xi_{N},\mathcal{N}\xi_{N}}+\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\braket{\widetilde{\xi}_{N},H_{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{N}}-E_{N}}\lesssim\sqrt{N}+\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\braket{\widetilde{\xi}_{N},H_{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{N}}-E_{N}}.

(46)

In the following we show by a contradiction argument that

\displaystyle\liminf_{N}\left\{\braket{\widetilde{\xi}_{N},H_{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{N}}-E_{N}\right\}=\infty.

(47)

For this purpose let us assume that Eq. (
47
) is violated, i.e. we assume that there exists a subsequence
N_{j}
and a constant
C>0
such that
\sup_{j}\braket{\widetilde{\xi}_{N_{j}},H_{N_{j}}\widetilde{\xi}_{N_{j}}}-E_{N_{j}}\leq C
. Let us complete this subsequence to a proper sequence by defining
\xi^{\prime}_{N}:=\widetilde{\xi}_{N}
in case
N=N_{j}
for some
j
and
\xi^{\prime}_{N}:=\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N}
otherwise. Clearly
\xi^{\prime}_{N}
is a sequence of approximate ground states, see Eq. (
40
), and as such
\xi^{\prime}_{N}
satisfies complete (BEC) by the results in
[
20
]
, i.e.
\braket{\xi^{\prime}_{N},\mathcal{N}\xi^{\prime}_{N}}=o_{N\rightarrow\infty}(N)
. This is however a contradiction to

\displaystyle\braket{\xi^{\prime}_{N_{j}},\mathcal{N}\xi^{\prime}_{N_{j}}}=\braket{\widetilde{\xi}_{N_{j}},\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{N_{j}}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}N,

which concludes the proof of Eq. (
47
). Combining Eq. (
46
) and Eq. (
47
) yields

\displaystyle\braket{\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N},\mathcal{N}\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N}}\leq C\sqrt{N},

for a suitable constant
C>0
. Applying again Lemma
21
for the state
\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N}
and
M:=K\sqrt{N}
, we obtain states
\Phi_{N}
satisfying
\mathds{1}(\mathcal{N}\leq K\sqrt{N})\Phi_{N}=\Phi_{N}
and

\displaystyle\braket{\Phi_{N},H_{N}\Phi_{N}}\leq E_{N}+\frac{C}{1-\frac{2}{K\sqrt{N}}\braket{\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N},\mathcal{N}\Psi^{\mathrm{GS}}_{N}}}\leq E_{N}+\frac{C}{1-\frac{2C}{K}},

for a large enough
C>0
. Consequently
\Phi_{N}
is a sequence of approximate ground states for
K>2C
. Finally we notice that the states
\Phi_{N}
satisfy the chain of inequalities in Eq. (
44
) as well, and therefore

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle=O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right).

∎

3
First Order Upper Bound

It is the goal of this section to introduce a trial state
\Gamma
, which simultaneously annihilates the variables
c_{k}
for
k\neq 0
and
\psi_{\ell mn}
in case
(\ell,m,n)\neq 0
, at least in an approximate sense, which allows us to verify the upper bound on the ground state energy
E_{N}
in Theorem
4
. For the rest of this Section we specify the parameter
K
introduced above the definition of
\pi_{K}
in Eq. (
19
) as
K:=0
, which especially means that with
\eta_{ijk}:=(T-1)_{ijk,000}
we have

\displaystyle c_{k}

\displaystyle=a_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij}\eta_{ijk}\,a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3},

(48)

\displaystyle\psi_{ijk}

\displaystyle=a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}+\eta_{ijk}\,a_{0}^{3}.

(49)

In order to find a suitable state
\Gamma
, let
\eta_{ijk}:=(T-1)_{ijk,000}
and let us follow the strategy in
[
20
]
, respectively in the case of Bose gases with two-particle interactions see e.g.
[
3
,
7
,
14
]
, by defining the generator

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}:=\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk}\eta_{ijk}\,a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}

(50)

of a unitary group
U_{s}:=e^{s\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}-s\mathcal{G}}
and
U:=U_{1}
. As we show this Section, the unitary
U
has the property
U^{-1}c_{k}U\approx a_{k}
and
U^{-1}\psi_{ijk}U\approx a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}
in a suitable sense. Denoting with

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}(x_{1},\dots,x_{N}):=1

(51)

the constant function in
L^{2}_{\mathrm{sym}}\!\left(\Lambda^{N}\right)
, i.e.
a_{k}\Gamma_{0}=0
for
k\neq 0
, we observe that
\Gamma:=U\Gamma_{0}
is a suitable trial state for the (approximate) annihilation of
c_{k}
, given
k\neq 0
, and
\psi_{\ell mn}
, given
(\ell,m,n)\neq 0
. Note that the action of the unitary
U
introduces a three-particle correlation structure on the completely uncorrelated wave function
\Gamma_{0}
.

For the purpose of verifying that
U^{-1}\psi_{ijk}U
is approximately identical to
a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}
, we first apply Duhamel’s formula, which yields

\displaystyle U^{-1}a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}U=a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}-\int_{0}^{1}U_{-s}\big[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}\big]U_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{1}U_{-s}\big[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s.

(52)

Furthermore, note that we can write

\displaystyle\big[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}\big]=\eta_{ijk}a_{0}^{3}+(\delta_{1}\psi)_{ijk}+(\delta_{2}\psi)_{ijk}

using the definition

\displaystyle(\delta_{1}\psi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\sum_{j}\eta_{i_{\sigma_{1}}i_{\sigma_{2}}j}\mathds{1}(i_{\sigma_{3}}=0)\,a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4},

\displaystyle(\delta_{2}\psi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\sum_{j}\eta_{i_{\sigma_{1}}i_{\sigma_{2}}j}\mathds{1}(i_{\sigma_{3}}\neq 0)\,a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{i_{\sigma_{3}}}a_{0}^{3}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\sum_{jk}\eta_{i_{\sigma_{1}}jk}\,a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{i_{\sigma_{2}}}a_{i_{\sigma_{3}}}a_{0}^{3}.

Therefore we can identify the transformed operators
U^{-1}\psi_{ijk}U
as

\displaystyle U^{-1}\psi_{ijk}U\!

\displaystyle=\!a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}\!+\!\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}\!\left\{\!\big[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]\!-\!(\delta_{1}\psi)_{ijk}\!-\!(\delta_{2}\psi)_{ijk}\!-\!\eta_{ijk}a_{0}^{3}\!\right\}\!U_{s}\mathrm{d}s\!+\!\eta_{ijk}U^{-1}a_{0}^{3}U

\displaystyle=\!a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}+\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}\big[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{s}\mathrm{d}s-\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}(\delta_{1}\psi)_{ijk}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s-\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}(\delta_{2}\psi)_{ijk}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\int_{0}^{1}\int_{s}^{1}U_{-t}\eta_{ijk}\big[a_{0}^{3},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{t}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s,

(53)

where we have used Duhamel’s formula to express
U^{-1}\eta_{ijk}a_{0}^{3}U-U_{-s}\eta_{ijk}a_{0}^{3}U_{s}
. The following Lemma
5
demonstrates that we can treat the quantities
\delta_{1}\psi
and
\delta_{2}\psi
in Eq. (
53
) as error terms. In order to formulate Lemma
5
, recall the set
\mathcal{L}_{0}:=\{(0,0,0)\}
from Eq. (
18
) and let us define

\displaystyle A:=(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{9}\setminus\mathcal{L}_{0}=(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{9}\setminus\{(0,0,0)\},

and the potential energy
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}
of an operator valued three particle vector
\Theta_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\Theta):=\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime})\in A}(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\Theta_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}^{\dagger}\Theta_{i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}.

(54)

To keep the notation light, we will occasionally write
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\Theta_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}})
for
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\Theta)
with dummy indices
i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}
.

Lemma 5
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\delta_{1}\psi)

\displaystyle\leq CN^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{N}+1),

(55)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\delta_{2}\psi)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{N}+1)^{4}.

(56)

Furthermore,
\mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(\big[a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]\right)\leq CN^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5}
.

Proof.

Let us define
A_{j}
as the set of all
s
such that
(-s,j-s,0)\in A
and

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}:=9\sum_{s,t\in A_{j}}\eta_{-s(s-j)j}\overline{\eta_{-t(t-j)j}}(V_{N})_{-s(j-s)0,-t(j-t)0}.

Making use of the fact that
V_{N}\geq 0
, we obtain by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3})\in A}\!\!(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}(\delta_{1}\psi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}^{\dagger}(\delta_{1}\psi)_{i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\leq(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}\,a_{j}a_{j}^{\dagger}\leq N^{4}\left(\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}\right)\!(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1).

By Lemma
15
and the fact that
|(V_{N})_{-s(j-s)0,-t(j-t)0}|\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}
, we have
N^{4}\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}\lesssim CN^{\frac{1}{2}}
, which concludes the proof of Eq. (
55
). In order to verify Eq. (
56
), let us first define

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\delta}_{2,\sigma}\psi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}:=\sum_{jk}\eta_{i_{\sigma_{1}}jk}\,a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{i_{\sigma_{2}}}a_{i_{\sigma_{3}}}a_{0}^{3}.

Then we obtain, using the sign
V_{N}\geq 0
and a Cauchy-Schwarz estimate,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\delta_{2}\psi)\!\lesssim\!\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\widetilde{\delta}_{2,\sigma}\psi\right)\!+\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\delta_{2}\psi\!-\!\frac{1}{4}\!\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\!\widetilde{\delta}_{2,\sigma}\psi\right)\!=\!6\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\widetilde{\delta}_{2,\mathrm{id}}\psi\right)\!+\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\delta_{2}\psi\!-\!\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\widetilde{\delta}_{2,\sigma}\psi\right).

Proceeding as in the proof of Eq. (
55
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\delta_{2}\psi\!-\!\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\widetilde{\delta}_{2,\sigma}\psi\right)\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}.

Regarding the term
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\widetilde{\delta}_{2,\mathrm{id}}\psi\right)
, let us define

\displaystyle(G)_{i_{1}..i_{4},j_{1}..j_{4}}:=(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}(-j_{3}-j_{4}),j_{1}j_{2}(-i_{3}-i_{4})}\eta_{i_{3}i_{4}(-i_{3}-i_{4})}\overline{\eta_{j_{3}j_{4}(-j_{3}-j_{4})}}

for
(i_{1},i_{2},-j_{3}-j_{4})\in A
and
(j_{1},j_{2},-i_{3}-i_{4})\in A
, and
(G)_{i_{1}..i_{4},j_{1}..j_{4}}:=0
otherwise. Then

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\widetilde{\delta}_{2,\mathrm{id}}\psi\right)=\sum_{i_{1}..i_{4},j_{1}..j_{4}}(G)_{i_{1}..i_{4},j_{1}..j_{4}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{3}}a_{j_{4}}a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{4}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}a_{0}^{3}

\displaystyle=\!\!\!\!\sum_{i_{1}..i_{4},j_{1}..j_{4}}\!\!\!\!\!\!(G)_{i_{1}..i_{4},j_{1}..j_{4}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{4}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{3}}a_{j_{4}}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}a_{0}^{3}\!+\!2\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{i_{1}..i_{3},j_{1}..j_{3},k}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(G^{\prime})_{i_{1}..i_{3},j_{1}..j_{3}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{3}}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}a_{0}^{3}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\sum_{i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}}\!\!\!(G^{\prime\prime})_{i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}a_{0}^{3},

(57)

with
(G^{\prime})_{i_{1}..i_{3},j_{1}..j_{3}}:=\sum_{k}G_{i_{1}..i_{3}k,j_{1}..j_{3}k}
and
(G^{\prime\prime})_{i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}}:=\sum_{k_{1}k_{2}}G_{i_{1}i_{2}k_{1}k_{2},j_{1}j_{2}k_{1}k_{2}}
. In the following let us study the term involving
G^{\prime\prime}
, which is responsible for the largest contribution. Since
(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}(-k_{1}-k_{2}),j_{1}j_{2}(-k_{1}-k_{2})}=(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}0,j_{1}j_{2}0}
, see Eq. (
24
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}}\!\!\!\!(G^{\prime\prime})_{i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}a_{0}^{3}\!=\!\!\left(\sum_{k_{1}k_{2}}|\eta_{k_{1}k_{2}(-k_{1}-k_{2})}|^{2}\!\right)\!\!\!\sum_{i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}}\!\!\!\!(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}0,j_{1}j_{2}0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}a_{0}^{3}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \lesssim N^{-3}\sum_{i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}}\!\!\!\!(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}0,j_{1}j_{2}0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}a_{0}^{3}\lesssim N^{-3}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}a_{0}^{3}\leq(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2},

where we have used

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}}\!(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}0,j_{1}j_{2}0}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}\lesssim(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2},

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \sum_{k_{1}k_{2}}|\eta_{k_{1}k_{2}(-k_{1}-k_{2})}|^{2}\lesssim N^{-3},

see Lemma
15
. Proceeding similarly for the other terms in Eq. (
57
), concludes the proof of Eq. (
56
). Regarding the bound on
\mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(\big[a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]\right)
, let us identify

\displaystyle\big[a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]=\bigg\{\frac{3}{2}\mathds{1}(i_{1}\!=\!0)(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\,\mathbb{A}+3\mathds{1}(i_{1}=i_{2}=0)a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{i_{3}}\,\mathbb{A}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\mathds{1}(i_{1}=i_{2}=i_{3}=0)\,\mathbb{A}\bigg\}+\{\mathrm{Permutations}\},

where
\mathbb{A}:=\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk}\eta_{ijk}a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}
. Due to the sign
V_{N}\geq 0
and the permutations symmetry of
V_{N}
, as well as to the fact that there are
6
permutations of the set
\{1,2,3\}
, we can bound the operator
\mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(\big[a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]\right)
from above by

\displaystyle 6\,\mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(\frac{3}{2}\mathds{1}(i_{1}\!=\!0)(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\,\mathbb{A}+3\mathds{1}(i_{1}=i_{2}=0)a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{i_{3}}\,\mathbb{A}+\mathds{1}(i_{1}=i_{2}=i_{3}=0)\,\mathbb{A}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq 18\,\mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(\frac{3}{2}\mathds{1}(i_{1}\!=\!0)(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\,\mathbb{A}\right)+18\,\mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(3\mathds{1}(i_{1}=i_{2}=0)a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{i_{3}}\,\mathbb{A}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +18\,\mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(\mathds{1}(i_{1}=i_{2}=i_{3}=0)\,\mathbb{A}\right).

In the following we focus on
\mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(\mathds{1}(i_{1}\!=\!0)(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\,\mathbb{A}\right)
, the other terms can be treated in a similar fashion. By Lemma
15
we have
\mathbb{A}^{\dagger}\mathbb{A}\lesssim N^{-3}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mathcal{E}_{P}\!\left(\mathds{1}(i_{1}\!=\!0)(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\,\mathbb{A}\right)=\!\mathbb{A}^{\dagger}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(ij),(\ell m)\in A^{0}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(V_{N})_{0jk,0mn}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{m}a_{n}\mathbb{A}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \lesssim a_{0}^{2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}\mathbb{A}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\mathbb{A}\!=\!a_{0}^{2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!4)\mathbb{A}^{\dagger}\mathbb{A}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!4)\!\lesssim\!a_{0}^{2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}N^{-3}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{5}\!\leq\!N^{-1}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{5},

where
A^{0}
contains all pairs
(jk)
such that
(0jk)\in A
.
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
5
, we obtain that the trial state
\Gamma
defined below Eq. (
51
) has a potential energy
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\psi)
of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
, see the following Corollary
2
.

Corollary 2
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, such that
\left\langle\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\psi+\delta_{1}\psi)\Gamma\right\rangle\leq C
and

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\psi)\Gamma\right\rangle\leq C\sqrt{N}.

Proof.

Recall that we can express the transformed quantity
U^{-1}\psi_{ijk}U
by Eq. (
53
) as

\displaystyle U^{-1}\psi_{ijk}U\!

\displaystyle=\!a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}+\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}\big[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{s}\mathrm{d}s-\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}(\delta_{1}\psi)_{ijk}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s-\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}(\delta_{2}\psi)_{ijk}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\int_{0}^{1}\int_{s}^{1}U_{-t}\eta_{ijk}\big[a_{0}^{3},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{t}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s,

where we have used Duhamel’s formula to express
U^{-1}\eta_{ijk}a_{0}^{3}U-U_{-s}\eta_{ijk}a_{0}^{3}U_{s}
. Using the sign
V_{N}\geq 0
and Lemma
5
, we estimate using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}(\delta_{1}\psi)_{ijk}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right)\leq\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!U_{-s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\delta_{1}\psi)U_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s\!\leq\!CN^{\frac{1}{2}}\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!U_{-s}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{4}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s\!\leq\!C^{\prime}N^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}(\delta_{2}\psi)_{ijk}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right)\leq\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!U_{-s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\delta_{2}\psi)U_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s\!\leq\!CN^{\frac{1}{2}}\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!U_{-s}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{4}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s\!\leq\!C^{\prime}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{4}.

(58)

for suitable
C,C^{\prime}
, where we utilize Lemma
19
in order to estimate
U_{-s}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{4}U_{s}
. Similarly

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}\big[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right)\leq C^{\prime}N^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{5}

(59)

follows from Lemma
5
. Regarding the term in the last line of Eq. (
53
), we note that

\displaystyle\big[a_{0}^{3},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]^{\dagger}\big[a_{0}^{3},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]\lesssim N(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}

follows from an analogous argument as we have seen in the proof of Lemma
5
and

\displaystyle\sum_{(ijk),(\ell mn)\in A}\!\left(V_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\overline{\eta_{ijk}}\eta_{\ell mn}\lesssim N^{-2}

by Lemma
15
. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{s}^{1}U_{-t}\eta_{ijk}\big[a_{0}^{3},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{t}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{s}^{1}\!\!\!U_{-t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\eta_{ijk}\big[a_{0}^{3},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]\right)\!U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s\lesssim N^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3},

(60)

where we have used Lemma
19
again. Using
a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}\Gamma_{0}=0
in case
(ijk)\in A
, we obtain by Eq. (
53
) together with Eq. (
58
), Eq. (
59
) and Eq. (
60
) for a suitable constant
C

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\psi\right)\Gamma\right\rangle=\left\langle\Gamma_{0},\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(U^{-1}\psi U\right)\Gamma_{0}\right\rangle\leq CN^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\Gamma_{0},(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5}\Gamma_{0}\right\rangle=CN^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Analogously we obtain
\left\langle\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\psi+\delta_{1}\psi)\Gamma\right\rangle\leq C
.
∎

Regarding the variable
c_{k}=a_{k}+[a_{k},\mathcal{G}]
from Eq. (
48
), let us apply Duahmel’s formula

\displaystyle U^{-1}c_{k}U

\displaystyle=a_{k}-\int_{0}^{1}U_{-s}[a_{k},\mathcal{G}]U_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s+U^{-1}[a_{k},\mathcal{G}]U=a_{k}+\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{s}^{1}U_{-t}\left[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s,

(61)

where we have used
\left[a_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]=0
for
k\neq 0
and
[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}]=0
, which follows from the observation that
\eta_{ijk}=0
in case one of the indices in
\{i,j,k\}
is zero. The following Lemma
6
provides useful estimates on the quantity
\left[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]
. In order to formulate Lemma
6
let us define the kinetic energy of an operator valued one particle vector
\Theta_{k}
, written as
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(\Theta)
or
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(\Theta_{k})
with
k
being a dummy index, as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(\Theta):=\sum_{k}|k|^{2}\Theta_{k}^{\dagger}\Theta_{k}.

(62)

Lemma 6
.

For
m\geq 0
there exists a constant
C_{m}>0
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\left[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]\right)\leq C_{m}N^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}.

Proof.

Let us write the double commutator as
\left[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]=(\delta_{1}c)_{k}+(\delta_{2}c)_{k}+(\delta_{3}c)_{k}
, where

\displaystyle(\delta_{1}c)_{k}:

\displaystyle=(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\sum_{ij}|\eta_{ijk}|^{2}a_{k},

\displaystyle(\delta_{2}c)_{k}:

\displaystyle=(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\sum_{ij,j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\overline{\eta_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}\eta_{ijk}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{j^{\prime}}a_{k^{\prime}},

\displaystyle(\delta_{3}c)_{k}:

\displaystyle=\left[a_{0}^{3},(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}\right]\!\left(\sum_{ij}\eta_{ijk}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}\right)\!\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\overline{\eta_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}a_{i^{\prime}}a_{j^{\prime}}a_{k^{\prime}}\right).

By Lemma
15
it is clear that

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}|\eta_{ijk}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{t}\frac{1}{(|k|^{2}+|t|^{2})^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{4}|k|},

and therefore
