\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{1}c\right)=\sum_{k}|k|^{2}(\delta_{1}c)_{k}^{\dagger}\mathcal{N}^{2m}(\delta_{1}c)_{k}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{8}}\sum_{k\neq 0}a_{k}^{\dagger}\left((a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\right)^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2m}a_{k}\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\mathcal{N}^{2m+1}.

Using
J_{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3},p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}^{\prime}}:=\sum_{qq^{\prime}k}|k|^{2}\overline{\eta_{q^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}^{\prime}}\eta_{qp_{1}^{\prime}k}}\eta_{q^{\prime}p_{1}k}\eta_{qp_{2}p_{3}}
and
\widetilde{J}_{p_{2}p_{3},p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}^{\prime}}:=\sum_{p_{1}}J_{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3},p_{1}p_{2}^{\prime}p_{3}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{2}c\right)

\displaystyle=\left((a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\right)^{2}\sum_{jp^{\prime}n^{\prime},pj^{\prime}k^{\prime}}J_{jp^{\prime}n^{\prime},pj^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\,a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{p^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{n^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{N}+2)^{2m}a_{p}a_{j^{\prime}}a_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ +\left((a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\right)^{2}\sum_{p^{\prime}n^{\prime},j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\widetilde{J}_{p^{\prime}n^{\prime},j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\,a_{p^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{n^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2m}a_{j^{\prime}}a_{k^{\prime}}.

Utilizing the operator
X_{jk,j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}:=\sum_{q}|k|\eta_{qjk}\overline{\eta_{qj^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}
acting on
L^{2}(\Lambda)^{\otimes 2}
and the permutation operator
(S\Psi)(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}):=\Psi(x_{2},x_{1},x_{3})
acting on
L^{2}(\Lambda)^{\otimes 3}
, we can write

\displaystyle J=(1\otimes X^{\dagger})S(1\otimes X),

and
\widetilde{J}=X^{\dagger}X
. Consequently

\displaystyle\|J\|\leq\|S\|\,\|1\otimes X\|^{2}=\|X\|^{2}=\|\widetilde{J}\|.

By Lemma
15
we have
\|\widetilde{J}\|\leq CN^{-\frac{15}{2}}
for a suitable constant
C
. Consequently

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{2}c\right)\leq CN^{-\frac{15}{2}}\left((a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\right)^{2}(\mathcal{N}+2)^{3+2m}\leq CN^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\mathcal{N}+2)^{3+2m}.

Similarly one can show that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{3}c\right)\lesssim N^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\left[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]\right)=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{1}+\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{2}+\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{1}\right)+3\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{2}\right)+3\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\delta_{3}\right)\lesssim C_{m}N^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}.

∎

As a consequence of Lemma
6
, we obtain that the trial state
\Gamma
defined below Eq. (
51
) has a kinetic energy
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(c)
of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(1\right)
in the subsequent Corollary
3
. Since in the residual term
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
1
the term
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{N}}c\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(c\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}c\right)
appears, it will be convenient to estimate the expectation value in the state
\Gamma
of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)
for
m\geq 1
as well.

Corollary 3
.

Lt
\Gamma
be the state defined below Eq. (
51
) and
m\geq 0
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
, such that
\left\langle\Gamma,\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)\Gamma\right\rangle\leq\frac{C_{m}}{N}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
19
we have

\displaystyle U^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{2m}U=(U^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{m}U)^{\dagger}U^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{m}U\lesssim(\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)^{2}

and hence

\displaystyle U^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)U=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(U^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{m}U\,U^{-1}c\,U\right)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)U^{-1}c\,U\right),

where we have used that for operators
f_{k}
and
A,B
satisfying
A^{\dagger}A\leq CB^{\dagger}B
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(Af_{k}\right)\leq C\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(Bf_{k}\right).

Proceeding as in the proof of Corollary
2
, we obtain by Eq. (
61
) and Lemma
6

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)U^{-1}c\,U\right)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)a\right)+\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)U_{-t}\left[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]U_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ =\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)a\right)+\int_{0}^{1}U_{-t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(U_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)U_{t}\left[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]\right)U_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)a\right)+\int_{0}^{1}U_{-t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)\left[[a_{k},\mathcal{G}],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\right]\right)U_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)a\right)+N^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}U_{-t}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}U_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)a\right)+N^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}.

where we have made use of Lemma
19
again. Using
a_{k}\Gamma_{0}=0
for
k\neq 0
therefore yields

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left\langle\Gamma,\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)\Gamma\right\rangle=\left\langle\Gamma_{0},\!U^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)U\Gamma_{0}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle\Gamma_{0},\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left((\mathcal{N}^{m}+1)a\right)\Gamma_{0}\right\rangle+N^{-1}\left\langle\Gamma_{0},\!(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}\Gamma_{0}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{N}.

∎

Having Corollary
2
and Corollary
3
at hand, we are in a position to verify the upper bound on the ground state energy
E_{N}
in Theorem
4
.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Let
A:=(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{9}\setminus\{(0,0,0)\}
, and let
\Gamma
be the state defined below Eq. (
51
). Using Eq. (
27
) and Eq. (
21
), and the fact that
\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}=\left(V_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}
for index triples
(ijk),(\ell mn)\in A
, we obtain

\displaystyle H_{N}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+\lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{(ijk),(\ell mn)\in A}\left(V_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\psi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\psi_{\ell mn}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(3a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\sum_{\ell\neq 0}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)-\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(c\right)+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\psi)+\left(3a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}\sum_{\ell\neq 0}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)-\mathcal{E}.

By a symmetry argument it is clear that
\braket{\Gamma,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}\Gamma}=0
. Applying Corollary
2
as well as Corollary
3
, with
m=0
, yields for suitable constants
C>0

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\psi)\Gamma\right\rangle\leq C\sqrt{N},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left\langle\Gamma,\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(c\right)\Gamma\right\rangle\leq\frac{C}{N}.

Furthermore, observe that
N^{3}\lambda_{0,0}\leq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+C^{\prime}
by Lemma
15
for a suitable
C^{\prime}
. In order to estimate the final term
\braket{\Psi,\mathcal{E}\Psi}
, note that we have by Lemma
15
for
m\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\braket{\Gamma,\mathcal{N}^{m}\Gamma}=\braket{\Gamma_{0},U^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{m}U\Gamma_{0}}\lesssim\braket{\Gamma_{0},(\mathcal{N}+1)^{m}\Gamma_{0}}=1.

(63)

Using Lemma
3
together with the estimate from Corollary
3
for
m=0
and
m=1
, we therefore obtain
\left|\braket{\Psi,\mathcal{E}\Psi}\right|\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{3}}
.
∎

4
Refined Correlation Structure

Utilizing the set of operators defined in Eq. (
25
) and Eq. (
26
), we where able to identify the ground state energy
E_{N}
up to errors of the magnitude
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}(\sqrt{N})
in the previous Sections
2
and
3
. It is the purpose of this Section to obtain a higher resolution of the energy, which especially captures the subleading term proportional to
\sqrt{N}
in the asymptotic expansion of
E_{N}
, using a more refined correlation structure compared to the one introduced in Subsection
1.1
. On a technical level, the new correlation structure is implemented by the new set of operators
d_{k}
and
\xi_{ijk}
defined below in Eq. (
66
) and Eq. (
67
), which constitute a refined version of the operators
c_{k}
and
\psi_{ijk}
respectively. Writing the operator
H_{N}
in terms of
d_{k}
and
\xi_{ijk}
will then allow us to verify the lower bound from Theorem
1
in Subsection
4.2
and the corresponding upper bound in Section
5
.

The approach presented in Sections
2
and
3
fails to capture the correct term of order
\sqrt{N}
for two reasons: (I) The following expression appearing in Eq. (
31
)

\displaystyle 3\!\sum_{|\ell|>K}\!\!\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}

(64)

is expected to lower the ground state energy by an amount proportional to
\sqrt{N}
, which is consistent with our estimate in Lemma
4
. (II) In the pursue of an upper bound on
E_{N}
we expressed the unitary conjugated variables
U^{-1}\psi_{ijk}U
as a sum of
a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}
and various error terms according to Eq. (
53
) as

\displaystyle U^{-1}\psi_{ijk}U\!

\displaystyle=\!a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}+\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}\big[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{s}\mathrm{d}s-\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}(\delta_{1}\psi)_{ijk}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s-\!\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\!\!\!U_{-s}(\delta_{2}\psi)_{ijk}U_{s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\int_{0}^{1}\int_{s}^{1}U_{-t}\eta_{ijk}\big[a_{0}^{3},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}\big]U_{t}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s.

(65)

While most of the terms appearing in Eq. (
65
) give a contribution of the order
o_{N\rightarrow}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
, the term
\delta_{1}\psi
is expected to increase the ground state energy by an amount proportional to
\sqrt{N}
, which is consistent with our estimate in Eq. (
55
). In order to extract the energy shift due to the expression in Eq. (
64
), we follow the strategy in Subsection
1.1
and introduce an additional two-particle correlation structure via a map acting on the two-particle space

\displaystyle T_{2}:L^{2}(\Lambda^{2})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\Lambda^{2})

in Eq. (
78
), which will give rise to the negative energy correction
-\mu(V)\sqrt{N}
from Theorem
1
. Regarding the energy shift associated with
\delta_{1}\psi
, it is a natural idea to include this term in the definition of our new operators
\xi_{ijk}
, giving rise to the positive energy correction
\gamma(V)\sqrt{N}
from Theorem
1
. However a computation in Eq. (
69
) demonstrates that the presence of
\delta_{1}\psi
produces new four-particle correlation terms of the form

\displaystyle a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.},

with
\{u,i,j,k\}
all different from zero, which are expected to lower the ground state energy by an amount proportional to
\sqrt{N}
. Again we extract the correlation energy by introducing a map, acting this time on the four-particle space

\displaystyle T_{4}:L^{2}(\Lambda^{4})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\Lambda^{4})

in Eq. (
71
), which gives rise to the negative energy correction
-\sigma(V)\sqrt{N}
from Theorem
1
.

In the following let
T:L^{2}(\Lambda^{3})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\Lambda^{3})
be the map constructed in Eq. (
20
), and for now let
T_{2}:L^{2}(\Lambda^{2})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\Lambda^{2})
and
T_{4}:L^{2}(\Lambda^{4})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\Lambda^{4})
be generic bounded permutation symmetric operators modelling the two-particle and four-particle correlation structure respectively. Following the approach in Section
2
, we are implementing many-particle counterparts to the transformations
T
,
T_{2}
and
T_{4}
as

\displaystyle d_{k}:

\displaystyle=a_{k}+\sum_{j,mn}(T_{2}-1)_{jk,mn}\,a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{m}a_{n}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij,\ell mn}(T-1)_{ijk,\ell mn}\,a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}

(66)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{6}\sum_{uij,v\ell mn}(T_{4}-1)_{uijk,v\ell mn}\,a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{v}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n},

\displaystyle\xi_{ijk}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell mn}(T)_{ijk,\ell mn}\,a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}+(\delta_{1}\psi)_{ijk}+\sum_{u,v\ell mn}(T_{4}-1)_{uijk,v\ell mn}\,a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{v}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}.

(67)

Note that
T_{2}
is not included in the definition of
\xi_{ijk}
, as it would only give contributions of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}(1)
. Using the Laplace operator
\Delta_{s}
acting on the space
L^{2}(\Lambda)^{\otimes s}
and the coefficients

\displaystyle(\chi)_{i_{1}..i_{4},j_{1}..j_{4}}:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\mathds{1}(j_{1}=..=j_{4}=i_{\sigma_{3}}=0)\eta_{i_{\sigma_{1}}i_{\sigma_{2}}i_{4}},

let us furthermore define the operators
X_{2}:=T_{2}^{\dagger}(-\Delta_{2})T_{2}+\Delta_{2}
and

\displaystyle X_{4}:

\displaystyle=\bigg(\left\{(-\Delta_{4}+4(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1))(T_{4}-1)+4(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)\chi\right\}+\mathrm{H.c.}\bigg)

(68)

\displaystyle+(T_{4}-1)^{\dagger}(-\Delta_{4})(T_{4}-1)+\left((T\otimes 1-1)^{\dagger}4(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)(T_{4}-1+\chi)+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)

\displaystyle+(T_{4}-1+\chi)^{\dagger}4(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)(T_{4}-1+\chi).

A straightforward computation, similar to the one in Eq. (
27
), reveals that up to excess terms involving
X_{2}
,
X_{4}
and an error term
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
, we can write the operator
H_{N}
as a sum of squares in the variables
d_{k}
and
\xi_{ijk}
according to

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}\!+\!\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\xi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\xi_{\ell mn}

\displaystyle\ =H_{N}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{jk,mn}(X_{2})_{jk,mn}\,a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{m}a_{n}+\frac{1}{24}\sum_{uijk,v\ell mn}(X_{4})_{uijk,v\ell mn}\,a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{v}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},

(69)

where the error
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
contains all the non-fully contracted products appearing in the squares

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2}(d_{k}-a_{k})^{\dagger}(d_{k}-a_{k}),\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}(\xi_{ijk}-\psi_{ijk})^{\dagger}(\xi_{\ell mn}-\psi_{ijk}).

(70)

In this context we define the fully contracted part of a product of monomials

\displaystyle\left(a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}\dots a_{i_{r}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{1}}\dots a_{j_{t}}\right)\left(a_{i^{\prime}_{1}}^{\dagger}\dots a_{i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}^{\dagger}a_{j^{\prime}_{1}}\dots a_{j^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}\right)

as
C_{j_{1}\dots j_{t},i^{\prime}_{1}\dots i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}\dots a_{i_{r}}^{\dagger}a_{j^{\prime}_{1}}\dots a_{j^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}
with
C_{j_{1}\dots j_{t},i^{\prime}_{1}\dots i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}
being the expectation of
a_{j_{1}}\dots a_{j_{t}}a_{i^{\prime}_{1}}^{\dagger}\dots a_{i_{r^{\prime}}}^{\dagger}
in the vacuum. For a term by term definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
see Eq. (
85
) in Subsection
4.1
.

In the following we want to choose
T_{4}
, such that the term
4(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)\chi
is cancelled in the expression
\{..\}
from Eq. (
68
), at least after symmetrization and projection onto the range of
Q^{\otimes 4}
, i.e. we define

\displaystyle T_{4}:=1-R_{4}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}Q^{\otimes 4}4(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)\chi=1-R_{4}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}Q^{\otimes 4}4(V_{N}\otimes 1)\chi,

(71)

where
\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}
is the orthogonal projection onto the subspace
L^{2}_{\mathrm{sym}}(\Lambda^{4})\subseteq L^{2}(\Lambda^{4})
and
R_{4}
is the pseudo-inverse of

\displaystyle Q^{\otimes 4}\left(-\Delta_{4}+4(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)\right)Q^{\otimes 4}=Q^{\otimes 4}\left(-\Delta_{4}+4(V_{N}\otimes 1)\right)Q^{\otimes 4}.

(72)

In order to obtain an improved representation of the operator
X_{4}
defined in Eq. (
68
), let us introduce the constants

\displaystyle\sigma_{N}:

\displaystyle=\frac{N^{4}}{6}\braket{(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)\chi u_{0}^{\otimes 4},(1-T_{4})u_{0}^{\otimes 4}},

(73)

\displaystyle=\frac{N^{4}}{24}\left\langle(T_{4}-1)u_{0}^{\otimes 4},\left(-\Delta_{4}+4V_{N}\otimes 1\right)(T_{4}-1)u_{0}^{\otimes 4}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\gamma_{N}:

\displaystyle=\frac{N^{4}}{6}\braket{(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)\chi u_{0}^{\otimes 4},\chi u_{0}^{\otimes 4}}=\frac{N^{4}}{6}\braket{(V_{N}\otimes 1)\chi u_{0}^{\otimes 4},\chi u_{0}^{\otimes 4}},

(74)

which allow us to write
\gamma_{N}-\sigma_{N}=\frac{N^{4}}{24}(X_{4})_{0000,0000}
. Furthermore, we define the three-particle state
\Theta
as

\displaystyle(\Theta)_{ijk}:

\displaystyle=4\left(\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}\frac{X_{4}}{24}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)_{0ijk,000},

(75)

\displaystyle(\Theta)_{0jk}:

\displaystyle=6\left(\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}\frac{X_{4}}{24}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)_{00jk,000}

for
\{i,j,k\}
all different from zero and
(\Theta)_{ijk}:=0
otherwise. According to the definition of
T_{4}
we have
Q^{\otimes 4}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}X_{4}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}P^{\otimes 4}=0
, and therefore

\displaystyle\frac{1}{24}\!\!\sum_{uijk,v\ell mn}\!\!\!\!(X_{4})_{uijk,v\ell mn}\,a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{v}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}\!=\!\frac{1}{24}\!\!\sum_{uijk,v\ell mn}\!\!\!\!(\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}X_{4}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}})_{uijk,v\ell mn}\,a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{v}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ =(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\,N^{-4}(\gamma_{N}-\sigma_{N})+\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right).

(76)

In order to understand the size of the term in Eq. (
76
) better, we are going to rewrite it in terms of the variables
\psi_{ijk}
defined in Eq. (
26
), respectively the variables

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}+\eta_{ijk}\,a_{0}^{3}

(77)

defined in Eq. (
26
) for the concrete choice
K:=0
, see Eq. (
49
), with the corresponding operator
T_{K=0}:=1+RV_{N}\pi_{0}
, see Eq. (
19
). Note that

\displaystyle(T_{K=0}^{-1})^{\dagger}\Theta=\Theta+2(\sigma_{N}-\gamma_{N})u_{0}^{\otimes 3},

and therefore

\displaystyle\frac{1}{24}\!\!\!\!\sum_{uijk,v\ell mn}\!\!\!\!\!\!(X_{4})_{uijk,v\ell mn}\,a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{v}a_{\ell}a_{m}a_{n}\!=\!N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\,(\sigma_{N}-\gamma_{N})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right).

In order to address the correlation term in Eq. (
64
), we make the concrete choice for
T_{2}

\displaystyle(T_{2}-1)_{\ell(-\ell),00}:=(T_{2}-1)_{00,\ell(-\ell)}:=3N\frac{\lambda_{0,\ell}}{|\ell|^{2}},

(78)

for
|\ell|>K
and
(T_{2}-1)_{jk,mn}:=0
otherwise, where
\lambda_{k,\ell}
is defined below Eq. (
31
). With

\displaystyle\mu_{N}:=\frac{N^{2}}{2}(X_{2})_{00,00},

(79)

this choice for a transformation
T_{2}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{jk,mn}(X_{2})_{jk,mn}\,a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{m}a_{n}=N^{-2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}\,\mu_{N}+\bigg(3Na_{0}^{2}\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}+\mathrm{H.c.}\bigg).

Summarizing what we have so far, allows us to write the operator
H_{N}
in terms of the new variables
d_{k}
and
\xi_{ijk}
as

\displaystyle\ H_{N}=\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}\!+\!\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left(\widetilde{V}_{N}\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\xi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\xi_{\ell mn}+N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\,(\gamma_{N}-\sigma_{N})-N^{-2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}\,\mu_{N}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ -\bigg(3Na_{0}^{2}\sum_{|\ell|>K}\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}+\mathrm{H.c.}\bigg)-\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}.

(80)

Defining the error term

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{*}:=3\!\left(a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}\!-\!N\right)\!a_{0}^{2}\!\sum_{|\ell|>K}\!\!\lambda_{0,\ell}\,a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}\!+\!9a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}\!\sum_{\ell,0<|k|\leq K}\!\!\lambda_{k,\ell}a_{\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k-\ell}^{\dagger}a_{k},

(81)

we obtain as a consequence of Eq. (
80
) the following Corollary
4
.

Corollary 4
.

Let
d_{k}
and
\xi_{ijk}
be as in Eq. (
66
) and Eq. (
67
), with
T_{2}
defined in Eq. (
78
) and
T_{4}
defined in Eq. (
71
),
\gamma_{N},\sigma_{N}
and
\mu_{N}
as in Eq. (
74
), Eq. (
73
) and Eq. (
79
), and let
\mathcal{E}_{*}
be as in Eq. (
81
),
\Theta
as in Eq. (
75
) and
\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}
as in Eq. (
77
). Furthermore, recall the definition of
\lambda_{0,0}
below Eq. (
29
) and
\mathbb{Q}_{K}
in Eq. (
32
). Then

\displaystyle H_{N}\geq(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\lambda_{0,0}+N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\,(\gamma_{N}-\sigma_{N})-N^{-2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}\,\mu_{N}+\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}

(82)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)+\left(\mathcal{E}_{*}+\mathcal{E}_{*}^{\dagger}\right)-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}.

Making use of the notation
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(d)
from Eq. (
62
) and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi)
from Eq. (
54
), we obtain in the case
K=0
the identity

\displaystyle H_{N}=\lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\!+\!(\gamma_{N}\!-\!\sigma_{N})N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\!-\!\mu_{N}N^{-2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(d)+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)+\left(\mathcal{E}_{*}+\mathcal{E}_{*}^{\dagger}\right)-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}.

(83)

Proof.

Using Eq. (
80
) and the definition of
\widetilde{V}_{N}
in Eq. (
21
), as well as the identities in Eq. (
29
) and Eq. (
30
), we obtain

\displaystyle H_{N}=\lambda_{0,0}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\!+\!(\gamma_{N}\!-\!\sigma_{N})N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\!-\!\mu_{N}N^{-2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}+\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left((1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\xi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\xi_{\ell mn}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)+\left(\mathcal{E}_{*}+\mathcal{E}_{*}^{\dagger}\right)-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}.

Since
\mathbb{Q}_{0}=0
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left((1-\pi_{K})V_{N}(1-\pi_{K})\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\xi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\xi_{\ell mn}\geq 0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}\left((1-\pi_{0})V_{N}(1-\pi_{0})\right)_{ijk,\ell mn}\xi_{ijk}^{\dagger}\xi_{\ell mn}=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi),

we immediately obtain Eq. (
82
), respectively Eq. (
83
).
∎

4.1
Analysis of the Error Terms

In the following we are providing an explicit representation of the error term
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
introduced in Eq. (
69
). For this purpose let us introduce the two-particle state
(\varphi_{2}^{0})_{jk}:=N(T_{2}-1)_{jk,00}
, the three-particle states
(\varphi^{0}_{3})_{ijk}:=\frac{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}(T-1)_{ijk,000}
and for
m\in(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{3}\setminus\{0\}

\displaystyle(\varphi_{3}^{m})_{ijk}:=\frac{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}(T-1)_{ijk,m00}+\frac{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}(T-1)_{ijk,0m0}+\frac{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}(T-1)_{ijk,00m},

and the four particle state
(\varphi^{0}_{4})_{uijk}:=\frac{N^{2}}{6}(T_{4}-1)_{uijk,0000}
as well as
(\varphi_{4})_{uijk}:=N^{2}(T_{4}-1+\chi)_{uijk,0000}
. Furthermore, let us introduce for
\varphi\in L^{2}(\Lambda^{s})
and
\psi\in L^{2}(\Lambda^{t})
with
s,t\geq 0
, and
\ell\leq\min\{s,t\}
, the operator

\displaystyle G_{\ell}(\varphi,\psi):=\mathrm{Tr}_{1\rightarrow\ell}\!\left[(-\Delta)_{x_{1}}\varphi\psi^{\dagger}\right]

(84)

acting on
L^{2}(\Lambda^{t-\ell})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\Lambda^{s-\ell})
. In coordinates, the operator is given by

\displaystyle\Big(G_{\ell}(\varphi,\psi)\Big)_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell},j_{1}\dots j_{t-\ell}}\!:=\sum_{k_{1}\dots k_{\ell}}\!|k_{1}|^{2}\varphi_{k_{1}\dots k_{\ell}i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell}}\overline{\psi}_{k_{1}\dots k_{\ell}j_{1}\dots j_{t-\ell}}.

Finally let
\widetilde{G}:=\mathrm{Tr}_{1\rightarrow 3}\!\left[\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1\varphi_{4}\varphi_{4}^{\dagger}\right]
. With this at hand we can write

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle=\!\!\sum_{(s,t,\ell,m,n)\in\mathcal{S}}C_{s,t,\ell}\underset{j_{1}\dots j_{t-\ell}}{\sum_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell}}}\!\!\Big(G_{\ell}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{t}^{n})\Big)_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell},j_{1}\dots j_{t-\ell}}a_{i_{s-\ell}}^{\dagger}\dots a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}\frac{a_{m}^{\dagger}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{s-1}a_{0}^{t-1}a_{n}}{N^{\frac{s+t}{2}}}a_{j_{1}}\dots a_{j_{t-\ell}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\sum_{i,j}\left(\widetilde{G}\right)_{i,j}a_{i}^{\dagger}\frac{(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}}{N^{4}}a_{j},

(85)

where
C_{s,t,\ell}:=\frac{(s-1)!(t-1)!}{(\ell-1)!(s-\ell)!(t-\ell)!}
and the set of allowed configurations
(s,t,\ell,m,n)\in\mathcal{S}
is defined by the rules
\ell\leq\min\{s,t\}
and
\ell<\max\{s,t\}
, where
2\leq s,t\leq 4
and
|n|,|m|\leq K
with
m=0
, respectively
n=0
, in case
s\neq 3
, respectively
t\neq 3
. Note that the criterion
\ell<\max\{s,t\}
makes sure that we only include non-fully contracted parts of the first product in Eq. (
70
) and the constant
C_{s,t,\ell}
counts the various different ways of contracting, while
\widetilde{G}
is the kernel associated with the non-fully contracted part of the second product in Eq. (
70
). Estimating the various terms appearing in Eq. (
85
) individually allows us to prove the following Lemma
7
.

Lemma 7
.

There exists a constant
C>0
and a function
\epsilon:[0,\infty)\longrightarrow(0,C)
satisfying
\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\epsilon(K)=0
, such that we have for
K
as in the definition of
\pi_{K}
below Eq. (
19
)

\displaystyle\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\leq CN^{-\frac{1}{4}}\!\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}\!\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}+1\right)^{2}\!\!c_{k}+CN^{-\frac{1}{4}}\!\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}+1\right)^{2}\!\!\left(\mathcal{N}+\sqrt{N}\right)+\epsilon(K)\mathcal{N}.

Proof.

In the following let
\tau\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Using the fact that
\|\frac{a_{m}^{\dagger}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{s-1}a_{0}^{t-1}a_{n}}{N^{\frac{s+t}{2}}}\|\leq 1
, there exists by Corollary
7
a constant
C>0
such that for
\delta>0
and
s,t\geq\ell+1

\displaystyle\pm\left(\underset{j_{1}\dots j_{t-\ell}}{\sum_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell}}}\!\!\Big(G_{\ell,\sigma,\tau}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{t}^{n})\Big)_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell},j_{1}\dots j_{t-\ell}}(\Phi_{\sigma,s})_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell}}^{\dagger}\frac{a_{m}^{\dagger}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{s-1}a_{0}^{t-1}a_{n}}{N^{\frac{s+t}{2}}}(\Phi_{\tau,t})_{j_{1}\dots j_{t-\ell}}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq C\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\tau,s-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{\ell}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{t}^{n})\mathcal{K}_{\tau,t-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\!\Bigg(\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}\left(\delta\mathcal{N}^{s-\ell-1}+\delta^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{t-\ell-1}\right)c_{k}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\left(\mathcal{N}+\sqrt{N}\right)\left(\delta\mathcal{N}^{s-\ell-1}+\delta^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{t-\ell-1}\right)\!\Bigg).

(86)

For
\tau=\sigma=0
, we have the improved bound on the left hand side of Eq. (
86
)

\displaystyle C\left\|G_{\ell}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{t}^{n})\right\|\left(\delta\mathcal{N}^{s-\ell}+\delta^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{t-\ell}\right).

(87)

In the case that either
s
or
t
is equal to
\ell
, e.g.
t=\ell
we obtain by Corollary
7

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \pm\left(\sum_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell}}\!\!\Big(G_{\ell,\sigma,\tau}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{t}^{n})\Big)_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell}}(\Phi_{\sigma,s})_{i_{1}\dots i_{s-\ell}}^{\dagger}\frac{a_{m}^{\dagger}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{s-1}a_{0}^{t-1}a_{n}}{N^{\frac{s+t}{2}}}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\tau,s-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{\ell}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{t}^{n})\right\|\!\Bigg(\delta^{-1}+\delta\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}\mathcal{N}^{s-\ell-1}c_{k}+\delta\left(\mathcal{N}+\sqrt{N}\right)\mathcal{N}^{s-\ell-1}\!\Bigg).

(88)

In order to obtain good estimates on the operator norms
\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,s-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{\ell}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{t}^{n})\mathcal{K}_{\tau,t-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|
, observe that we obtain by a Cauchy-Schwarz argument

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,s-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{\ell}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{t}^{n})\mathcal{K}_{\tau,t-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,s-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{\ell}(\varphi_{s}^{m},\varphi_{s}^{m})\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,s-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\tau,t-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{\ell}(\varphi_{t}^{n},\varphi_{t}^{n})\mathcal{K}_{\tau,t-\ell}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|},

i.e. it is enough to control the norm of the symmetric ones. In the following we choose
\sigma=\tau=\frac{1}{2}
, except for the case
s=t=2
where we choose
\sigma=\tau=0
. By Lemma
15
and Lemma
18
we obtain for a suitable
C>0

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{1}(\varphi_{4}^{0},\varphi_{4}^{0})\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq CN^{-\frac{3}{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{1}(\varphi_{3}^{m},\varphi_{3}^{m})\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq CN^{-1},

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{2}(\varphi_{4}^{0},\varphi_{4}^{0})\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq CN^{-\frac{3}{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{2}(\varphi_{3}^{m},\varphi_{3}^{m})\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq CN^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{3}(\varphi_{4}^{0},\varphi_{4}^{0})\mathcal{K}_{\frac{1}{2},1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq CN^{-1},

\displaystyle\left\|G_{3}(\varphi_{3}^{m},\varphi_{3}^{m})\right\|\leq CN.

Furthermore,
\left\|G_{1}(\varphi_{2}^{0},\varphi_{2}^{0})\right\|\leq\epsilon
in case
K
is large enough and
\left\|G_{2}(\varphi_{2}^{0},\varphi_{2}^{0})\right\|\leq C\sqrt{N}
, as well as
\left\|\widetilde{G}\right\|\leq CN^{-1}
. Choosing
\delta:=N^{\frac{t-s}{4}}
, and combining the estimates on the operator norms with Eq. (
86
), respectively Eq. (
87
), and Eq. (
88
) concludes the proof by Eq. (
85
).
∎

Following the ideas in the proof of Lemma
7
, we can furthermore compare the operator
\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}
with the corresponding operator
\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}
in the variables
d_{k}
defined in Eq. (
66
). This is the content of the subsequent Lemma
8
.

Lemma 8
.

Let
0\leq\tau<\frac{1}{4}
. Then there exists
K_{0},C>0
such that for
K\geq K_{0}
, with
K
as in the definition of
\pi_{K}
below Eq. (
19
),

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\leq C\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}+CN^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{N}^{3}+CN^{\tau}.

Proof.

By Lemma
20
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\leq C\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+CN^{\tau}.

Furthermore, we have by Cauchy-Schwarz the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\leq 2\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}+\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}(d_{k}-c_{k})^{\dagger}(d_{k}-c_{k}).

Similar to the definition of
G_{\ell}
in Eq. (
84
) let us introduce

\displaystyle G^{\prime}_{\ell}:

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}_{1\rightarrow\ell}\!\left[(-\Delta)_{x_{1}}\varphi^{0}_{2}(\varphi^{0}_{2})^{\dagger}\right],

\displaystyle G^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}:

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}_{1\rightarrow\ell}\!\left[(-\Delta)_{x_{1}}\varphi_{4}^{0}(\varphi_{4}^{0})^{\dagger}\right].

A similar computation as in Eq. (
85
) together with a Cauchy-Schwarz estimate yields

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}(d_{k}-c_{k})^{\dagger}(d_{k}-c_{k})\leq CG^{\prime}_{2}\frac{(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{2}}+C\sum_{i,j}(G^{\prime}_{1})_{i,j}a_{i}^{\dagger}\frac{(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}}{N^{2}}a_{j}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +C\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}\underset{j_{1}\dots j_{4-\ell}}{\sum_{i_{1}\dots i_{4-\ell}}}\!\!\Big(G^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}\Big)_{i_{1}\dots i_{4-\ell},j_{1}\dots j_{4-\ell}}a_{i_{4-\ell}}^{\dagger}\dots a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}\frac{(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}}{N^{4}}a_{j_{1}}\dots a_{j_{4-\ell}}.

for a suitable constant
C>0
. Utilizing the estimates in Lemma
18
we obtain that
|G^{\prime}_{2}|\lesssim 1
,
\|G^{\prime}_{2}\|\lesssim\frac{1}{K^{2}}
,
|G^{\prime\prime}_{4}|\lesssim N^{\tau-\frac{1}{2}}\leq 1
and
\|G^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}\|\lesssim N^{\tau-2}\leq N^{-\frac{3}{2}}
for
\ell\leq 3
. Consequently there exists a
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}(d_{k}-c_{k})^{\dagger}(d_{k}-c_{k})\leq C+\frac{C}{K^{2}}\mathcal{N}+CN^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}.

Using
\mathcal{N}\leq\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}
and
\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}\leq\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}
we therefore obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\leq\frac{C}{K^{2}}\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}+C\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}+CN^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{N}^{3}+CN^{\tau}.

Choosing
K
large enough such that
\frac{C}{K^{2}}<1
concludes the proof.
∎

Before we come to the proof of the lower bound in Theorem
1
in the following Subsection
4.2
, we are going to derive sufficient estimates on

\displaystyle\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}

in Lemma
10
. Lemma
9
is an auxiliary result required in the proof of Lemma
10
.

Lemma 9
.

Then there exists a
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2}\tilde{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\tilde{c}_{k}\leq C\left(\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+\mathcal{N}+1\right),

where we define
\tilde{c}_{k}:=a_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij}(T-1)_{ijk,000}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{3}
.

Proof.

Similar to Eq. (
140
), we can write

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|k|^{2}(c_{k}\!-\!\tilde{c}_{k})^{\dagger}(c_{k}\!-\!\tilde{c}_{k})\!=\!\sum_{I,I^{\prime}\neq 0}\!\left(\!f^{I,I^{\prime}}X^{I,I^{\prime}}_{0}\!+\!\sum_{k\neq 0}\!g^{I,I^{\prime}}_{k}a_{k}^{\dagger}X^{I,I^{\prime}}_{1}a_{k}\!+\!\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{1}^{\dagger}\left(\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{1,1}\!+\!\mathrm{H.c.}\!\right)\!\Phi_{1}\!\right),

where
f^{I,I^{\prime}},g_{k}^{I,I^{\prime}},\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{1,1},X^{I,I^{\prime}}_{0}
and
X^{I,I^{\prime}}_{1}
are defined below Eq. (
140
) for the concrete choice
s:=0
and
\Phi_{1}
is defined at the start of the proof of Lemma
20
. Using
I,I^{\prime}\neq 0
, we obtain the improved estimates
\pm X^{I,I^{\prime}}_{0}\lesssim N^{2}\mathcal{N}
and
\pm X^{I,I^{\prime}}_{1}\lesssim N^{2}\mathcal{N}
. Consequently

\displaystyle\pm(f^{I,I^{\prime}}X^{I,I^{\prime}}_{0}+\mathrm{H.c.})\lesssim\mathcal{N},

\displaystyle(\sum_{k\neq 0}\!g^{I,I^{\prime}}_{k}a_{k}^{\dagger}X^{I,I^{\prime}}_{1}a_{k}+\mathrm{H.c.})\lesssim N^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{N}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\pm\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{1}^{\dagger}\left(\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{(I,I^{\prime})}_{1,1}\!+\!\mathrm{H.c.}\!\right)\!\Phi_{1}\lesssim\sum_{k}|k|^{2}a_{k}^{\dagger}\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}a_{k}\lesssim\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+\mathcal{N}+1,

where we have used Lemma
20
in the last estimate.
∎

Having Lemma
9
at hand, we are in a position to verify the subsequent Lemma
10
.

Lemma 10
.

Let
0\leq\gamma<\frac{1}{4}
. Then there exists a
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\pm\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)\leq N^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}+N^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathcal{N}+CN^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Proof.

Let us define for
ijk\neq 0

\displaystyle\zeta_{ijk}:

\displaystyle=\!\frac{1}{24}\!\Big(\!\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}4(\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)(T_{4}\!-\!1\!+\!\chi)\Big)_{0ijk,0000}\!\!=\frac{1}{24}\!\Big(\!\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}4(V_{N}\otimes 1)(T_{4}\!-\!1\!+\!\chi)\Big)_{0ijk,0000},

(89)

\displaystyle\zeta_{k}:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{24}\Big(\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}4(T^{\dagger}\widetilde{V}_{N}\otimes 1)(T_{4}-1+\chi)\Big)_{00k(-k),0000}.

Then we have the decomposition

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)

\displaystyle=4\left(\sum_{ijk\neq 0}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)+6\left(\sum_{k\neq 0}\overline{\zeta_{k}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{2}\,a_{k}a_{-k}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right).

(90)

Note that
\zeta_{k}=\frac{1}{24}\Big(\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}4(V_{N}\otimes 1)(T_{4}-1+\chi)\Big)_{00k(-k),0000}
for
|k|>K
. Using the regularity of
V
and the bounds derived in Lemma
15
and Lemma
18
, we observe that we have
|N^{3}\zeta_{k}|\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\frac{|k|^{2}}{N}\right)^{-1}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\pm\left(\!\sum_{k\neq 0}\!\overline{\zeta_{k}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{2}a_{k}a_{-k}\!+\!\mathrm{H.c.}\!\!\!\right)\!\!\lesssim\!\epsilon\!\sum_{k\neq 0}\!|k|^{2\tau}\!a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\!+\!\epsilon^{-1}N^{\frac{1}{2}-\tau}\!\!\lesssim\!\epsilon\sum_{k\neq 0}\!|k|^{2\tau}\!c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\!+\!\epsilon\mathcal{N}\!+\!\epsilon N^{\tau}\!+\!\epsilon^{-1}N^{\frac{1}{2}-\tau},

where we have used Lemma
20
. Choosing
\epsilon
of the order
N^{-\frac{1}{4}}
and
\tau=\frac{1}{2}
concludes the analysis of the the second term in Eq. (
90
). Regarding the first term, we use the definition of
\tilde{c}_{k}
from Lemma
9
, in order to identify
\sum_{ijk}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}a_{0}
as

\displaystyle\sum_{ijk}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{i}a_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}a_{0}-\sum_{ijk}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{i}a_{j}(\tilde{c}_{k}-a_{k})a_{0}+\sum_{ijk}\zeta_{ijk}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}(T-1)_{ijk,000}

\displaystyle\ \ \ =\sum_{ijk}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{i}a_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}a_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{ijk,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(T-1)_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k,000}a_{i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{j^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4\dagger}a_{0}^{4}a_{i}a_{j}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -2\sum_{ijk,i^{\prime}}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(T-1)_{i^{\prime}jk,000}a_{i^{\prime}}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4\dagger}a_{0}^{4}a_{i}.

(91)

In the following we are going to verify that the most significant term
\sum_{ijk}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{i}a_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}a_{0}
in Eq. (
91
) satisfies the desired bound. By Cauchy-Schwarz, we have for
\epsilon>0

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{ijk}\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{i}a_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}a_{0}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)\leq\epsilon\sum_{k}|k|^{2}\tilde{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\tilde{c}_{k}+\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{k}\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\left|\sum_{ij}\zeta_{ijk}a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\ =\epsilon\sum_{k}|k|^{2}\tilde{c}_{k}^{\dagger}\tilde{c}_{k}+\epsilon^{-1}G^{(0)}X+\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}}(G^{(1)})_{i,i^{\prime}}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{i^{\prime}}X+\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(G^{(2)})_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}a_{i}^{\dagger}a^{\dagger}_{j}Xa_{j^{\prime}}a_{i^{\prime}}

with
G^{(0)}:=N^{5}\sum_{ijk}\frac{|\zeta_{ijk}|^{2}}{|k|^{2}}
,
G^{(1)}_{i,i^{\prime}}:=N^{5}\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}\sum_{jk}\frac{|\zeta_{ijk}|^{2}+\overline{\zeta_{ijk}}\zeta_{jik}}{|k|^{2}}
and
G^{(2)}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}:=N^{5}\sum_{k}\frac{\zeta_{ijk}\zeta_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k}}{|k|^{2}}
, and
X:=N^{-5}a_{0}^{4\dagger}a_{0}a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}
. Using again the regularity of
V
and Lemma
15
, as well as the bounds on
T_{4}
from Lemma
18
, yields

\displaystyle|\zeta_{ijk}|\leq CN^{-\frac{7}{2}}\delta_{i+j+k=0}\left(1+\frac{|i|^{2}+|j|^{2}+|k|^{2}}{N}\right)^{-3},

and therefore
|G^{(0)}|\lesssim 1
and
\|G^{(1)}\|\lesssim N^{-\frac{3}{2}}
. The choice
\epsilon:=N{-\frac{1}{4}}
then yields

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-1}G^{(0)}X+\epsilon^{-1}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}}G^{(1)}_{i,i^{\prime}}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{i^{\prime}}X\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{4}}+N^{-\frac{5}{4}}\mathcal{N}.

Finally
\|G^{(2)}\|\leq N^{-\frac{3}{2}}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}G^{(2)}_{ij,i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}a_{i}^{\dagger}a^{\dagger}_{j}Xa_{j^{\prime}}a_{i^{\prime}}\lesssim N^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{N}^{2}\leq N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}.

This concludes the proof together with Lemma
9
.
∎

4.2
Proof of the lower Bound in Theorem
1

In this Subsection, we are going to verify the lower bound in Theorem
1
making use of the sequence of states
\Phi_{N}
constructed in Corollary
1
, which simultaneously satisfies

\displaystyle\mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\leq C\sqrt{N}\right)\Phi_{N}=\Phi_{N},

\displaystyle\braket{\Phi_{N},H_{N}\Phi_{N}}\leq E_{N}+C,

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle\leq C\sqrt{N}.

Starting point for our investigations is then the lower bound

\displaystyle H_{N}\geq\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}+(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\lambda_{0,0}+N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\,(\mu_{N}-\sigma_{N})-N^{-2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}\,\mu_{N}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)+\left(\mathcal{E}_{*}+\mathcal{E}_{*}^{\dagger}\right)-\widetilde{\mathcal{E}},

see Eq. (
82
). Given
\epsilon>0
, assume that
K
is large enough such that the function
\epsilon(K)
from Lemma
7
satisfies
\epsilon(K)\leq\epsilon
. Making use of the fact that

\displaystyle\ \ \ \mathds{1}\!\left(\mathcal{N}\leq C\sqrt{N}\right)\Phi_{N}=\Phi_{N},

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle\lesssim\sqrt{N},

we immediately obtain for
C
and
\widetilde{C}
large enough

\displaystyle|\braket{\Phi_{N},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\Phi_{N}}|\!\leq\!CN^{-\frac{1}{4}}\!\left\langle\!\Phi_{N},\!\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Phi_{N}\!\right\rangle\!+\!\epsilon\!\left\langle\Phi_{N},\mathcal{N}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle\!+\!CN^{\frac{1}{4}}\!\leq\!\widetilde{C}N^{\frac{1}{4}}\!+\!\epsilon\!\left\langle\Phi_{N},\mathcal{N}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle\!.

Similarly we obtain by Lemma
10
and Lemma
4
for suitable
C,\widetilde{C}>0

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|\braket{\Phi_{N},\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)\Phi_{N}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-\frac{1}{4}}\left(\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}c_{k}^{\dagger}c_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle+\sqrt{N}\right)+CN^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq\widetilde{C}N^{\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left|\braket{\Phi_{N},\left(\mathcal{E}_{*}+\mathcal{E}_{*}^{\dagger}\right)\Phi_{N}}\right|\leq CN^{\frac{1}{4}}.

By Lemma
2
we furthermore obtain for
\tau,\epsilon>0
and
K
large enough, and a suitable
C>0
,

\displaystyle\braket{\Phi_{N},\left((a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\lambda_{0,0}+\mathbb{Q}_{K}\right)\Phi_{N}}\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N-\epsilon\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle-C.

Moreover we note that we have by Lemma
17

\displaystyle\braket{\Phi_{N},N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\,(\sigma_{N}-\gamma_{N})\Phi_{N}}\leq(\sigma_{N}-\gamma_{N})+|\sigma_{N}-\gamma_{N}|\braket{\Phi_{N},\left(1-N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\right)\Phi_{N}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq\sigma_{N}-\gamma_{N}+|\sigma_{N}-\gamma_{N}|\left\langle\Phi_{N},\frac{\mathcal{N}}{N}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle\leq(\sigma(V)-\gamma(V))\sqrt{N}+o_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(N^{\frac{1}{4}}\right),

and similarly
\braket{\Phi_{N},N^{-2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}\,\mu_{N}\Phi_{N}}\leq\mu(V)\sqrt{N}+o_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(1\right)
. Finally by Lemma
8

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi_{N},\mathcal{N}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2\tau}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle\leq C\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle+CN^{\tau}.

Choosing
\tau<\frac{1}{4}
and
\epsilon<\frac{1}{2C}
concludes the proof, since

\displaystyle E_{N}+C

\displaystyle\geq\braket{\Phi_{N},H_{N}\Phi_{N}}\geq\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N+\left(\gamma-\sigma-\mu\right)\!\sqrt{N}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ -CN^{\frac{1}{4}}+\left(1-2C\epsilon\right)\left\langle\Phi_{N},\sum_{k}|k|^{2}d_{k}^{\dagger}d_{k}\Phi_{N}\right\rangle.

5
Second Order Upper Bound

It is the goal of this Section to introduce a trial state
\Phi
, which simultaneously annihilates the variables
d_{k}
for
k\neq 0
and
\xi_{\ell mn}
in case
(\ell,m,m)\neq 0
, at least in an approximate sense. We are then going to use this trial state
\Phi
to verify the upper bound in Theorem
1
. For the rest of this Section we specify the parameter
K
introduced above the definition of
\pi_{K}
in Eq. (
19
) as
K:=0
. In order to find
\Phi
, we define
\alpha_{jk}:=(T_{2}-1)_{jk,00}
and
\beta_{uijk}:=(T_{4}-1)_{uijk,0000}
, and the generator

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2}:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{jk}\alpha_{jk}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a^{2}_{0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{4}:=\frac{1}{24}\sum_{uijk}\beta_{uijk}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a^{4}_{0}

(92)

of a unitary group
W_{s}:=e^{s(\mathcal{G}_{2}+\mathcal{G}_{4})^{\dagger}-s(\mathcal{G}_{2}+\mathcal{G}_{4})}
and
W:=W_{1}
. Applying Duhamel’s formula, we can express
W^{-1}a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}W
as

\displaystyle W^{-1}a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}W=a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\!-\!\int_{0}^{1}W_{-s}[a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}_{4}]W_{s}\mathrm{d}s\!+\!\int_{0}^{1}W_{-s}[a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}\!-\!\mathcal{G}_{2}]W_{s}\mathrm{d}s.

(93)

Furthermore, note that we can write

\displaystyle[a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}_{4}]=\sum_{u}\beta_{ui_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+(\delta\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}},

(94)

where we define the error term

\displaystyle(\delta\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}:=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\sum_{jk}\beta_{i_{\sigma_{2}}i_{\sigma_{3}}jk}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{i_{\sigma_{1}}}a_{0}^{4}+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{3}}\sum_{ijk}\beta_{i_{\sigma_{3}}ijk}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{i_{\sigma_{1}}}a_{i_{\sigma_{2}}}a_{0}^{4}.

Therefore we can write the transformed operators
W^{-1}\xi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}W
as

\displaystyle W^{-1}\xi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}W=(\psi\!+\!\delta_{1}\psi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\!-\!\int_{0}^{1}W_{s}^{-1}(\delta\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}W_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s\!+\!\int_{0}^{1}W_{-s}[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}\!-\!\mathcal{G}_{2}]W_{s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{s}W_{t}^{-1}\left[\sum_{u}\beta_{ui_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4},\,\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{G}_{2}-\mathcal{G}_{4}\right]W_{t}\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\int_{0}^{1}W_{-s}[\xi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}-a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\,\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{G}_{2}-\mathcal{G}_{4}]W_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s.

(95)

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}
defined in Eq. (
54
). The following Lemma
11
provides sufficient bounds on the various error terms appearing in Eq. (
95
).

Lemma 11
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\delta\xi)\leq C(\mathcal{N}+1)^{6}.

(96)

Furthermore, we have
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left([a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}\!-\!\mathcal{G}_{2}]\right)\leq C(\mathcal{N}+1)^{6}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left([\xi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}-a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\,\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{G}_{2}-\mathcal{G}_{4}]\right)\leq C(\mathcal{N}+1)^{6},

(97)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\left[\sum_{u}\beta_{ui_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4},\,\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{G}_{2}-\mathcal{G}_{4}\right]\right)\leq C(\mathcal{N}+1)^{6}.

(98)

Proof.

Let us define

\displaystyle(\delta_{1}\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{jk}\beta_{i_{2}i_{3}jk}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{i_{1}}a_{0}^{4},

\displaystyle C_{N}:

\displaystyle=\sup_{i_{1}}\sum_{jk,i_{2}i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\left|(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\beta_{i_{2}i_{3}jk}\beta_{i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}jk}\right|\lesssim N^{-5},

where we have used Lemma
18
to estimate
C_{N}
. Applying Cauchy-Schwarz yields

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime})\in A}(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}(\delta_{1}\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}^{\dagger}(\delta_{1}\xi)_{i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\leq C_{N}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\left(\sum_{i_{1}}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{1}}\right)(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}.

(99)

Using the fact that
C_{N}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\left(\sum_{i_{1}}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{1}}\right)\leq C_{N}N^{5}\lesssim 1
, we observe that the quantity in Eq. (
99
) is bounded by the right hand side of Eq. (
96
). Let us furthermore define

\displaystyle(\delta_{2}\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}:=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{ijk}\beta_{i_{3}ijk}a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{k}^{\dagger}a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{0}^{4}.

In the following we want to distinguish between the cases
A^{\prime}:=\{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})\in A:i_{1},i_{2}\neq 0\}
and
A^{\prime\prime}:=A\setminus A^{\prime}
, leading to the definition

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{N}:=\sup_{i_{1}i_{2}}\sum_{ijk,i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\mathds{1}\big((i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}),(i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3})\in A^{\prime}\big)\left|(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\beta_{i_{3}ijk}\beta_{i^{\prime}_{3}ijk}\right|\lesssim N^{-5},

\displaystyle C^{\prime\prime}_{N}:=\sup_{i_{1}i_{2}}\sum_{ijk,i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\mathds{1}\big((i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}),(i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3})\in A^{\prime\prime}\big)\left|(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\beta_{i_{3}ijk}\beta_{i^{\prime}_{3}ijk}\right|\lesssim N^{-\frac{13}{2}},

where we have again used Lemma
18
. Applying Cauchy-Schwarz leads to the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime})\in A}(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}(\delta_{2}\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}^{\dagger}(\delta_{2}\xi)_{i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\leq C_{N}^{\prime}N^{4}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5}+C_{N}^{\prime\prime}N^{6}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3},

which is bounded by the right hand side of Eq. (
96
). Finally we use that
V_{N}
is permutation symmetric and non-negative, and therefore the left hand side of Eq. (
96
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ 6\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime})\in A}(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}(\delta_{1}\xi+\delta_{2}\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}^{\dagger}(\delta_{1}\xi+\delta_{2}\xi)_{i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq 12\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime})\in A}\!\!\!\!\!\!(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}(\delta_{1}\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}^{\dagger}(\delta_{1}\xi)_{i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}+12\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime})\in A}\!\!\!\!\!\!(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}(\delta_{2}\xi)_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}^{\dagger}(\delta_{2}\xi)_{i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}.

Regarding the term
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left([a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}\!-\!\mathcal{G}_{2}]\right)
, let us analyse the term involving the commutator with
\mathcal{G}_{2}
, the terms involving
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}
can be analysed in a similar fashion as has been done in Lemma
5
. We compute

\displaystyle[a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}_{2}]=\alpha_{i_{2}i_{3}}a_{i_{1}}a_{0}^{2}+\sum_{u}\alpha_{ui_{3}}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}+\{\mathrm{Permutations}\}.

(100)

In order to analyse the first term on the right hand side of Eq. (
100
), let us define
D_{N}:=\sup_{i_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\left|(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\alpha_{i_{2}i_{3}}\alpha_{i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\right|
and note that
D_{N}\lesssim N^{-3}
by Lemma
18
. Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime})\in A}(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\left(\alpha_{i_{2}i_{3}}a_{i_{1}}a_{0}^{2}\right)^{\dagger}\alpha_{i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}a_{i^{\prime}_{1}}a_{0}^{2}\leq D_{N}N^{3}\lesssim 1.

Regarding the second term on the right hand side of Eq. (
100
), we use again the split
A=A^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime\prime}
and define

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{N}:=\sup_{i_{1}i_{2}}\sum_{u,i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\mathds{1}\big((i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}),(i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3})\in A^{\prime}\big)\left|(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\alpha_{ui_{3}}\alpha_{ui^{\prime}_{3}}\right|\lesssim N^{-\frac{5}{2}},

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{N}:=\sup_{i_{1}i_{2}}\sum_{u,i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\mathds{1}\big((i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}),(i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3})\in A^{\prime\prime}\big)\left|(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i^{\prime}_{1}i^{\prime}_{2}i^{\prime}_{3}}\alpha_{ui_{3}}\alpha_{ui^{\prime}_{3}}\right|\lesssim N^{-4},

where we have used Lemma
18
. Consequently

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}),(i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime})\in A}(V_{N})_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},i_{1}^{\prime}i_{2}^{\prime}i_{3}^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{u}\alpha_{ui_{3}}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\sum_{u}\alpha_{ui_{3}}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\right)\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}+1,

and therefore
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left([a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\mathcal{G}_{2}]\right)\leq 12\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\alpha_{i_{2}i_{3}}a_{i_{1}}a_{0}^{2}\right)+12\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\sum_{u}\alpha_{ui_{3}}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}}\right)\lesssim(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}
. The inequalities in Eq. (
97
) and Eq. (
98
) can be verified in similarly.
∎

With Lemma
11
at hand, we show in the subsequent Corollary
5
that after conjugation with the unitary
W
, the potential energy of the operators
\xi_{ijk}
is comparable to the potential energy of
(\psi+\delta_{1})_{ijk}
.

Corollary 5
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, such that

\displaystyle W^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi)W\leq C\,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\psi+\delta_{1}\psi\right)+C\,(\mathcal{N}+1)^{6}.

Proof.

Using the sign
V_{N}\geq 0
, we obtain by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the representation of
W^{-1}\xi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}W
in Eq. (
95
) the estimate

\displaystyle W^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi)W=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(W^{-1}\xi W\right)\leq 5\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\psi\!+\!\delta_{1}\right)+5\int_{0}^{1}W_{s}^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\delta\xi\right)W_{s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ +5\int_{0}^{1}W_{-s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left([a_{i}a_{j}a_{k},\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}\!-\!\mathcal{G}_{2}]\right)\mathrm{d}sW_{s}

\displaystyle\ \ +\frac{5}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{s}W_{t}^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\left(\left[\sum_{u}\beta_{ui_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}a_{u}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4},\,\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{G}_{2}-\mathcal{G}_{4}\right]\right)W_{t}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ +5\int_{0}^{1}W_{-s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\left([\xi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}-a_{i_{1}}a_{i_{2}}a_{i_{3}},\,\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{G}_{2}-\mathcal{G}_{4}]\right)W_{s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\psi\!+\!\delta_{1}\right)\!+\!\int_{0}^{1}\!W_{-s}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{6}W_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s\!+\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{0}^{s}\!W_{-t}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{6}W_{t}\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s\!\lesssim\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}\!\left(\psi\!+\!\delta_{1}\right)\!+\!(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{6},

where we have first used Lemma
11
and subsequently Lemma
19
in the last line.
∎

Regarding the variable
d_{k}
, Duhamel’s formula yields for
k\neq 0

\displaystyle W^{-1}d_{k}W\!=\!c_{k}\!+\!\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{0}^{s}\!W_{-t}\left[\left[a_{k},G_{2}\!+\!G_{4}\right],\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]W_{t}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s\!+\!\int_{0}^{1}\!W_{-s}\left[d_{k}\!-\!a_{k},G_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!G_{4}^{\dagger}\right]W_{s}\mathrm{d}s.

(101)

Recall the kinetic energy of an operator valued one particle vector
\Theta_{k}
defined in Eq. (
62
). Then the following Lemma
12
provides sufficient bounds on the various error terms appearing in Eq. (
101
).

Lemma 12
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, such that for
m\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\left[d_{k}-a_{k},G_{2}^{\dagger}+G_{4}^{\dagger}\right]\right)\leq\frac{C}{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\left[\left[a_{k},G_{2}+G_{4}\right],\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}+\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]\right)\leq\frac{C}{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}.

Proof.

Let us compute as an example for
k\neq 0

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \left[\left[a_{k},G_{4}\right],\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]=\frac{1}{24}\sum_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\left[a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger}a^{4}_{0},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{24}\sum_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}\left[a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]a^{4}_{0}+\frac{1}{24}\sum_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}\left[a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger}a^{4}_{0}

\displaystyle\ \ +\frac{1}{24}\sum_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\left[a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger}a^{4}_{0}+\frac{1}{24}\sum_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger}\left[a^{4}_{0},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right],

and let us focus on the term

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}\left[a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]a^{4}_{0}=\frac{1}{6}\!\sum_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3},j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\overline{\beta_{i_{3}j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}a_{j_{1}}a_{j_{2}}a_{j_{3}}.

Defining

\displaystyle C_{N}:=\sum_{k,i_{1}i_{2},j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}|k|^{2}\left|\sum_{i_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\overline{\beta_{i_{3}j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}}\right|^{2}\lesssim N^{-\frac{19}{2}},

where we have used Lemma
18
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\sum_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\beta_{ki_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}\mathcal{N}^{m}a_{i_{1}}^{\dagger}a_{i_{2}}^{\dagger}\left[a_{i_{3}}^{\dagger},\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]a^{4}_{0}\right)\lesssim N^{-\frac{19}{2}}\left((a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\right)^{2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}\leq N^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}.

The other estimates in Lemma
12
can be verified similarly.
∎

Similar to Corollary
5
, we show in the following Corollary
6
that, after conjugation with the unitary
W
, the kinetic energy of the operators
d_{k}
is comparable to the one of
c_{k}
.

Corollary 6
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, such that for
m\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle W^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}d\right)W\leq C\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(c\right)+C\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)+\frac{C}{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5+2m}.

Proof.

By Lemma
19
we have
W^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{2m}W=(W^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{m}W)^{*}W^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{m}W\lesssim\mathcal{N}^{2m}+1
, hence

\displaystyle W^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}d\right)W=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(W^{-1}\mathcal{N}^{m}W\,W^{-1}d\,W\right)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(W^{-1}d\,W\right)+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}W^{-1}d\,W\right).

Following the ideas in Corollary
5
, we estimate using Eq. (
101
)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}W^{-1}d\,W\right)\leq 3\,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{3}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{0}^{s}\!W_{-t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\left[\left[a_{k},G_{2}\!+\!G_{4}\right],\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{4}\right]\right)W_{t}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\!3\int_{0}^{1}W_{-s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}\left[d_{k}\!-\!a_{k},G_{2}^{\dagger}\!+\!G_{4}^{\dagger}\right]\right)W_{s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)\!+\!\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{1}\!\int_{0}^{s}\!\!W_{-t}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{5+2m}W_{t}\mathrm{d}s\!+\!\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{1}\!\!W_{-s}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{5}W_{s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\!\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)\!+\!\frac{1}{N}(\mathcal{N}\!+\!1)^{5+2m},

where we have first used Lemma
12
and subsequently Lemma
19
in the last line.
∎

Before we come to the proof of the upper bound in Theorem
1
, we are showing in the following Lemma
13
that even without a unitary conjugation, the kinetic energy of
c_{k}
is comparable with the one of
d_{k}
. The price for dropping the unitary
W
is that we obtain an order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\right)
pre-factor in front of the excess term
(\mathcal{N}+3)^{3+2m}
, instead of a pre-factor of the order
O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(1\right)
.

Lemma 13
.

There exists a constant
C>0
, such that for
m\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)\leq C\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}d\right)+C\sqrt{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3+2m}.

Proof.

Note that we can write
c_{k}
as

\displaystyle c_{k}=d_{k}-2\sum_{j}\alpha_{jk}\alpha_{jk}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}-4\sum_{uij}\beta_{uijk}a^{\dagger}_{u}a^{\dagger}_{i}a^{\dagger}_{j}a_{0}^{4},

and therefore

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)\leq 3\,\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}d\right)+12\,\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\sum_{j}\alpha_{jk}\,\mathcal{N}^{m}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}\right)+48\,\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\sum_{uij}\beta_{uijk}\,\mathcal{N}^{m}a^{\dagger}_{u}a^{\dagger}_{i}a^{\dagger}_{j}a_{0}^{4}\right).

Defining the constant
C_{N}:=\sum_{jk}|k|^{2}|\alpha_{jk}|^{2}\lesssim N^{-\frac{3}{2}}
, which follows from Lemma
18
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\sum_{j}\alpha_{jk}\,\mathcal{N}^{m}a_{j}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{2}\right)\leq C_{N}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{m+1}\lesssim\sqrt{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{m+1}.

Similarly we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\sum_{uij}\beta_{uijk}\,\mathcal{N}^{m}a^{\dagger}_{u}a^{\dagger}_{i}a^{\dagger}_{j}a_{0}^{4}\right)\lesssim\sqrt{N}(\mathcal{N}+3)^{3}\lesssim\sqrt{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}.

∎

Proof of the upper bound in Theorem
1
.

Let us define the trial state
\Phi:=W\Gamma
, where
\Gamma
is the state defined below Eq. (
51
), and recall the representation of
H_{N}
in Eq. (
83
)

\displaystyle\braket{\Phi,H_{N}\Phi}=\lambda_{0,0}N^{3}\braket{\Phi,N^{-3}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\Phi}\!+\!(\gamma_{N}\!-\!\sigma_{N})\braket{\Phi,N^{-4}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{4}a_{0}^{4}\Phi}\!-\!\mu_{N}\braket{\Phi,N^{-2}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{0}^{2}\Phi}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(d)\Phi}+\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi)\Phi}+2\mathfrak{Re}\left\langle\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{*}\Phi\right\rangle-\left\langle\Phi,\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\Phi\right\rangle

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\left\langle\Phi,\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)\Phi\right\rangle.

By Lemma
19
and the fact that
\pm\left(N^{-m}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{m}a_{0}^{m}-1\right)\lesssim N^{-1}\mathcal{N}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\braket{\Phi,N^{-3}(a_{0}^{\dagger})^{3}a_{0}^{3}\Phi}-1\right|\lesssim N^{-1}\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{N}\Phi}=N^{-1}\braket{\Gamma,W^{-1}\mathcal{N}W\Gamma}\lesssim N^{-1}\braket{\Gamma,(\mathcal{N}+1)\Gamma}\lesssim N^{-1},

see Eq. (
63
) for the last estimate. Making use of Lemma
7
, Lemma
4
and Lemma
10
yields

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{*}\Phi\right\rangle\right|\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{4}}\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(c)\Phi}+N^{\frac{1}{4}}\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{N}\Phi},

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\!\Phi,\left(\sum_{ijk,\ell mn}(\Theta)_{ijk}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{ijk}^{\dagger}\,a_{0}^{\dagger}a_{0}^{4}+\mathrm{H.c.}\right)\Phi\!\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{4}}\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(c)\Phi}+N^{-\frac{1}{4}}\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{N}\Phi}+N^{\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle\left|\!\left\langle\Phi,\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\Phi\!\right\rangle\right|\!\lesssim\!N^{-\frac{1}{4}}\left\langle\!\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\!\left(\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}\!+\!1\right)c\right)\Phi\!\right\rangle\!+\!N^{\frac{1}{4}}\left\langle\!\Phi,\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{N}}\!+\!1\right)^{2}\!\!\left(\mathcal{N}\!+\!\sqrt{N}\right)\!\Phi\right\rangle\!+\!\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{N}\Phi}.

Observe that
\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{N}^{m}\Phi}\lesssim 1
and furthermore we have by Lemma
13
and Corollary
6

\displaystyle\left\langle\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)\Phi\right\rangle\lesssim\left\langle\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}d\right)\Phi\right\rangle+\sqrt{N}=\left\langle\Gamma,W^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}d\right)W\Gamma\right\rangle+\sqrt{N}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\left\langle\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(c\right)\Gamma\right\rangle+\left\langle\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{K}\!\left(\mathcal{N}^{m}c\right)\Gamma\right\rangle+\sqrt{N}\lesssim\sqrt{N},

where we used Corollary
3
in the last estimate. Putting together what we have so far, and utilizing Lemma
17
, yields

\displaystyle\braket{\Phi,H_{N}\Phi}\!=\!\frac{1}{6}b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)N\!+\!\big(\gamma(V)\!-\!\sigma(V)\!-\!\mu(V)\big)\sqrt{N}\!+\!\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(d)\Phi}\!+\!\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi)\Phi}\!+\!O_{N\rightarrow\infty}\!\left(\!N^{\frac{1}{4}}\!\right)\!.

Using Corollary
5
, Corollary
6
, Corollary
2
and Corollary
3
, we further have

\displaystyle 0\leq\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi)\Phi}=\braket{\Gamma,W^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\xi)W\Gamma}\lesssim\braket{\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{P}}(\psi+\delta_{1}\psi)\Gamma}+1\lesssim 1,

\displaystyle 0\leq\braket{\Phi,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(d)\Phi}=\braket{\Gamma,W^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(d)W\Gamma}\lesssim\braket{\Gamma,\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}(c)\Gamma}+\frac{1}{N}\lesssim\frac{1}{N}.

∎

6
Analysis of the Scattering Coefficients

This Section is devoted to the study of the variational problems in the definition of
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
in Eq. (
4
) and the definition of
\sigma(V)
in Eq. (
12
) as well the study of their corresponding minimizers
\omega
and
\eta
. Especially we want to compare
\gamma(V),\mu(V)
and
\sigma(V)
defined in Eq. (
5
), Eq. (
8
) and Eq. (
12
) with
\gamma_{N},\mu_{N}
and
\sigma_{N}
defined in Eq. (
73
), Eq. (
79
) and Eq. (
74
), see Lemma
17
. The proof will be based on the observation that the
N
-dependent quantities can be seen as a counterpart on the three dimensional torus
\Lambda
to the
N
-independent quantities defined in terms of variational problems on the full space
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Similarly we will compare in Lemma
16
the modified scattering length
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
, which can be expressed in terms of the minimizer
\omega
as

\displaystyle b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(1-\omega)V\,\mathrm{d}x,

see
[
20
]
, with its counterpart on the torus
\Lambda
defined below Eq. (
29
) as

\displaystyle 6\lambda_{0,0}=\braket{u_{0}u_{0}u_{0},(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N})u_{0}u_{0}u_{0}}=\left(V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}\right)_{000,000}.

The proof of Lemma
16
is based on the observation that
(1-\omega)V=V-\omega V
is the full space counterpart to the renormalized potential
V_{N}-V_{N}RV_{N}
.

In the following Lemma
14
we want to derive properties of
\mathcal{Q}
defined in Eq. (
11
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\varphi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\left\{2\big|\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\varphi(x)\big|^{2}+\mathbb{V}(x)\!\left|\frac{f(x)}{\mathbb{V}(x)}-\varphi(x)\right|^{2}\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x

and its minimizers. For this purpose it will be useful to introduce for a given cut-off parameter
\ell
and a smooth function
\chi:\mathbb{R}^{6}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
function with
\chi(x)=1
for
|x|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{3}
and
\chi(x)=0
for
|x|_{\infty}>\frac{1}{2}
, the modified function

\displaystyle f_{\ell}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}):=V(x_{1},x_{2})\chi(\ell^{-1}x_{2},\ell^{-1}x_{3})\omega(x_{2},x_{3}).

Furthermore, we define the corresponding functional, acting on
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{9})
, as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}(\varphi):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\left\{2\big|\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\varphi(x)\big|^{2}+\mathbb{V}(x)\!\left|\frac{f_{\ell}(x)}{\mathbb{V}(x)}-\varphi(x)\right|^{2}\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x,

and
\sigma_{\ell}(V):=\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}(0)-\inf_{\varphi\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{9})}\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}(\varphi)
.

Lemma 14
.

There exists a unique minimizer
\eta
of the functional
\mathcal{Q}
in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{9})
, and
\eta
satisfies the point-wise bounds
0\leq\eta\leq\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}f
and
\sigma(V)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}f(x)\eta(x)\mathrm{d}x
, as well as

\displaystyle(-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}+\mathbb{V})\eta=f

in the sense of distributions. Furthermore,
\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}
has a unique minimizer
\eta_{\ell}
, and
\eta_{\ell}
satisfyies
0\leq\eta_{\ell}\leq\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}f_{\ell}
and
\sigma_{\ell}(V)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}f_{\ell}(x)\eta_{\ell}(x)\mathrm{d}x
, as well as
(-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}+\mathbb{V})\eta_{\ell}=f_{\ell}
and

\displaystyle\sigma(V)=\lim_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\sigma_{\ell}(V).

Proof.

Following the proof of
[
20
]
, we observe that since
\mathcal{Q}(0)<\infty
, there exists a minimizing sequence
\varphi_{n}
for
\mathcal{Q}
with
\sup_{n}\|\nabla\varphi_{n}\|<\infty
and
\sup_{n}\left\|\sqrt{\mathbb{V}}\left(\frac{f}{\mathbb{V}}-\varphi_{n}\right)\right\|<\infty
, and therefore there exists by Banach-Alaoglu a subsequence
\varphi_{n}
and an element
\eta\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{9})
, such that
\nabla\varphi_{n}\rightharpoonup\nabla\eta
,
\sqrt{\mathbb{V}}\left(\frac{f}{\mathbb{V}}-\eta\right)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{9})
and
\sqrt{\mathbb{V}}\left(\frac{f}{\mathbb{V}}-\varphi_{n}\right)\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\mathbb{V}}\left(\frac{f}{\mathbb{V}}-\eta\right)
. We observe that
\eta
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{Q}
, since

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\eta)\!=\!2\|\mathcal{M}_{*}\!\nabla\eta\|^{2}\!+\!\Big\|\!\sqrt{\mathbb{V}}\Big(\frac{f}{\mathbb{V}}\!-\!\eta\Big)\!\Big\|^{2}\!\!\!\leq\!\liminf_{n}\!\left\{\!2\|\mathcal{M}_{*}\!\nabla\varphi_{n}\|^{2}\!+\!\Big\|\!\sqrt{\mathbb{V}}\Big(\frac{f}{\mathbb{V}}\!-\!\varphi_{n}\Big)\!\Big\|^{2}\!\right\}\!=\!\liminf_{n}\mathcal{Q}(\varphi_{n}).

Computing
0=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{Q}(\eta+t\varphi)
for
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{9})
immediately gives in the sense of distributions

\displaystyle(-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}+\mathbb{V})\eta=f,

and computing
0=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{Q}(\eta+t\eta)
yields
\sigma(V)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}f(x)\eta(x)\mathrm{d}x
. Regarding the uniqueness, we note that
\varphi\mapsto\|\mathcal{M}_{*}\!\nabla\varphi\|^{2}
is strictly convex on
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{9})
, and therefore
\mathcal{Q}
is strictly convex too. Consequently the minimizer
\eta
is unique. Using that
\frac{f}{\mathbb{V}}\geq 0
, we obtain
\mathcal{Q}(|\varphi|)\leq\mathcal{Q}(\varphi)
for all
\varphi\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{9})
and by the uniqueness of the minimizer
\eta=|\eta|\geq 0
. For the purpose of obtaining an upper bound on
\eta
, we observe that
\frac{1}{\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla}f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{9})
and define the functional

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\left\{2\Big|\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\varphi(x)+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla}f(x)\Big|^{2}+\mathbb{V}(x)\!\left|\varphi(x)\right|^{2}\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x.

Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=\mathcal{Q}(\varphi)+\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}(0)-\mathcal{Q}(0)
, we observe that
\eta
is the unique minimizer of
\widetilde{Q}
. It is furthermore clear that
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\min\{\varphi,\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}f\})\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)
, and therefore we obtain by the uniqueness of minimizer for
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}

\displaystyle\eta=\min\!\left\{\eta,\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}f\right\}\!\leq\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}f.

The properties of
\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}
can verified analogously.

In order to compare
\sigma(V)
with
\sigma_{\ell}(V)
, let us first verify the point-wise bounds

\displaystyle\eta_{\ell}\leq\eta\leq\eta_{\ell}+\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}(f-f_{\ell}).

(102)

For this purpose we introduce the additional functionals
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell}(\varphi):

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\left\{2\Big|\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\varphi-\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\eta\Big|^{2}+\mathbb{V}\!\left|\varphi-\eta+\frac{f-f_{\ell}}{\mathbb{V}}\right|^{2}\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}(\varphi):

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\left\{2\Big|\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\varphi-\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla\eta_{\ell}+\frac{1}{2\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla}f-\frac{1}{2\mathcal{M}_{*}\nabla}f_{\ell}\Big|^{2}+\mathbb{V}\!\left|\varphi-\eta_{\ell}\right|^{2}\!\right\}\mathrm{d}x.

By a straightforward computation, we observe that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell}(\varphi)=\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}(\varphi)+\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell}(0)-\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}(0)
and similarly
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}(\varphi)=\mathcal{Q}(\varphi)+\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}(0)-\mathcal{Q}(0)
. Therefore
\eta_{\ell}
is the unique minimizer of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell}
, and since
f(x)\geq f_{\ell}(x)
we further have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell}\!\left(\min\left\{\varphi,\eta\right\}\right)\leq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell}(\varphi).

Consequently
\eta_{\ell}\leq\eta
. The second inequality in Eq. (
102
) follows analogously, using that
\eta
is the unique minimizer of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}
and that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}\!\left(\min\left\{\varphi,\eta_{\ell}+\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}(f-f_{\ell})\right\}\right)\leq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}(\varphi).

Using the fact that
|f(x)-f_{\ell}(x)|\lesssim\frac{\mathds{1}(|x|>\frac{\ell}{3})}{|x|^{4}}
, see
[
20
]
, we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}(f-f_{\ell})(x)=\frac{\Gamma\!\left(\frac{9}{2}\right)}{28\pi^{\frac{9}{2}}\mathrm{det}[M_{*}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}\frac{f(y)-f_{\ell}(y)}{|\mathcal{M}_{*}^{-1}(x-y)|^{7}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\int_{|y|\geq\frac{\ell}{3}}\frac{1}{|y|^{4}|x-y|^{7}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{\ell},

i.e.
\frac{1}{-2\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{*}}}(f-f_{\ell})
converges point-wise to zero and consequently
\eta_{\ell}
converges point-wise to
\eta
by Eq. (
102
). Using Fatou’s Lemma and
f_{\ell}(x)\eta_{\ell}(x)\geq 0
, as well as the fact that
f_{\ell}
converges point-wise to
f
, therefore yields

\displaystyle\sigma(V)\!=\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}f(x)\eta(x)\mathrm{d}x\leq\liminf_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{9}}f_{\ell}(x)\eta_{\ell}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\liminf_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\sigma_{\ell}(V)\leq\limsup_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\sigma_{\ell}(V)\leq\sigma(V),

where we have used in the last inequality that
f_{\ell}\eta_{\ell}\leq f\eta
by Eq. (
102
).
∎

Before we can compare the modified scattering length
b_{\mathcal{M}}(V)
with its counterpart on the torus in Lemma
16
, we need the following auxiliary result Lemma
15
.

Lemma 15
.

Recall the definition of the coefficients
\lambda_{k,\ell}
below Eq. (
29
) and the definition of
T
in Eq. (
20
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that
|\lambda_{k,\ell}|\leq\frac{C}{N^{2}}\left(1+\frac{|\ell|^{2}}{N}\right)^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\left|(T-1)_{ijk,\ell 00}\right|\leq\frac{C\,\mathds{1}(i+j+k=\ell)}{N^{2}(|i|^{2}+|j|^{2}+|k|^{2})}\left(1+\frac{|i|^{2}+|j|^{2}+|k|^{2}}{N+|\ell|^{2}}\right)^{-2}.

(103)

Proof.

In order to verify Eq. (
103
), we observe that for
|\ell|\leq K
,
\nabla^{n}(T-1)e^{i\ell x}=\nabla^{n}RV_{N}e^{i\ell x}
can be written as the sum of terms of the form

\displaystyle Q^{\otimes 3}\nabla^{k_{1}}\!(V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}\dots Q^{\otimes 3}\nabla^{k_{m}}\!(V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}\,\nabla^{a}R^{1-b}V_{N}e^{i\ell x},

(104)

where the coefficients satisfy
k_{1}+\dots+k_{m}+2m+a+2b=n
and either (I) that
b=1
, (II) that
b=0
and
a=1
or (III) that
b=0
,
a=0
and
m\geq 1
as well as
k_{m}=0
. In the following we are going to verify individually for the three cases (I)-(III) that the Fourier transform of the expression in Eq. (
104
) has an
L^{\infty}
bound of the order
N^{-2}(\sqrt{N}+|\ell|)^{n-2}
for
n\geq 2
, which immediately implies Eq. (
103
). Let us first of all state the useful bounds

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{Q^{\otimes 3}\nabla^{k}(V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\|Q^{\otimes 3}\nabla^{k}(V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}\|}\lesssim\sqrt{N}^{\frac{k}{2}+1},

(105)

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{Q^{\otimes 3}\nabla^{k}(V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}}e^{iK\cdot X}\right\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\braket{e^{iK\cdot X},Q^{\otimes 3}\nabla^{k}(V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}e^{iK\cdot X}}}\lesssim\sqrt{N}^{\frac{k}{2}-2}

(106)

for
k\geq 0
. Regarding the case (I), we obtain immediately by Eq. (
106
) and Eq. (
106
)

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle e^{iK\cdot X},Q^{\otimes 3}\nabla^{k_{1}}\!(V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}\dots Q^{\otimes 3}\nabla^{k_{m}}\!(V_{N})Q^{\otimes 3}\,\nabla^{a}V_{N}e^{i\ell x}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{N}^{k_{1}+\dots+k_{m}+2m-4}\big(\sqrt{N}+|\ell|\big)^{a}\leq N^{-2}(\sqrt{N}+|\ell|)^{n-2}.

Since the case (II) is similar to the case (III), let us directly have a look at the case (III), where we use the fact that
\|\sqrt{Q^{\otimes 3}V_{N}Q^{\otimes 3}}R\nabla\|\lesssim 1
to obtain
